4:T8ce,## What

Upgrades `@biomejs/biome` from **2.4.16 → 2.5.1** (latest stable) across the entire workspace and reformats the tree to 2.5.1's canonical style.

## Why

The workspace had a **biome version skew**: `package.json` files declared a mix of `2.4.16`, `2.5.0`, and `^2.4.14`, while `bun.lock` (and therefore CI's `bun install --frozen-lockfile`) resolved **2.4.16**. Because PR CI lint is path-gated (it only lints changed files), 2.5.x-formatted files merged via PRs that didn't touch them could slip in and then fail a full repo `bun run lint`. Pinning one version everywhere removes that whole class of intermittent lint drift.

## Changes

**Commit 1 — version bump (config only):**
- Pin `@biomejs/biome` to `2.5.1` in all **97** `package.json` files
- Regenerate `bun.lock` (resolves `2.4.16` → `2.5.1`)
- Bump `$schema` to `2.5.1` in all **68** `biome.json` configs
- Migrate the deprecated `linter.rules.recommended: true` → `linter.rules.preset: "recommended"` in **50** configs (behavior-identical; removes the 2.5.x deprecation diagnostic)

**Commit 2 — mechanical reformat:**
- `biome check --write` under the 2.5.1 toolchain (formatting + organizeImports only — **no logic changes**). The bulk of the diff is the trailing-comma behavior change between 2.4 and 2.5.

## Verification

- **Per-package lint green at 2.5.1:** serial sweep of all no-write first-party packages → **51 pass, 0 needing fixes, 0 residual lint errors**.
- `packages/core` typechecks clean (the reformat is format + import-ordering, which cannot change types).
- All `biome.json` configs validate at 2.5.1 (no `deserialize`/deprecation diagnostics; verified core/agent/app).

## Notes for the reviewer

- This is a large (~1100-file) **mechanical** change. The meaningful review surface is **commit 1** (versions + config). Commit 2 is pure formatter output.
- It's a repo-wide reformat, so it can go stale against an active `develop`. If it can't fast-forward at merge time, the cleanest sync is to **regenerate** rather than rebase: `bun install` then `bunx @biomejs/biome check --write .` on the latest `develop`. (At open time it rebased onto `develop` with **zero** conflicts.)

a:Tc5c,## What & why

Closes the **deepest** part of #9967: "the Kotlin that runs on a phone is executed by
no test, on no device." Before this PR the entire `plugin-native-*` set had **zero**
Android instrumented tests; the only coverage drove the React views against a **mocked
`Capacitor.Plugins` bridge in desktop Chromium**, so the real Kotlin — the launcher's
Phone/SMS/Home/Assistant gating — ran untested.

Root cause of *why* it was untested: the native logic was welded to the Capacitor
`Plugin`/`Bridge`/`PluginCall` objects, which only exist inside a running app, so it
could not be asserted from `androidTest`. This PR fixes that at the source.

### Changes
- **`RoleStatus`** (added in the prior commit): the launcher/comms role read is
  extracted out of `SystemPlugin` into a pure, `Context`-taking object — `RoleManager`
  / `TelecomManager` / `PackageManager` / `Telephony` reads with **no** bridge
  dependency. `SystemPlugin.getStatus` just delegates, so runtime behavior is unchanged.
- **`RoleStatusInstrumentedTest`** now lives in the plugin's *own* `androidTest` source
  set (`@elizaos/capacitor-system`) and runs the real `RoleStatus.read()` against the
  device's actual system services — the first on-device test in the native set.
- **plugin `build.gradle`** gains `AndroidJUnitRunner` + `androidTest` deps so the
  plugin library is instrumentable **standalone** — no full-app build (voice/inference
  JNI, fused `.so`, omnivoice submodule) is needed to run it. That's the pattern the
  rest of the native-plugin × view matrix can follow.

## Evidence — real Pixel 6a (Android 16)

```
> Task :system:connectedDebugAndroidTest
Starting 3 tests on Pixel 6a - 16
Finished 3 tests on Pixel 6a - 16
BUILD SUCCESSFUL
testsuite ... tests="3" failures="0" errors="0" skipped="0"
```

The three tests prove the read returns **live** device state, not a stub:
- `read_reportsRealPackageNameFromContext` — packageName comes from the real `Context`.
- `read_enumeratesAllFourRolesFromRealRoleManager` — all 4 roles (home/dialer/sms/
  assistant) enumerate from the real `RoleManager`, each mapping to its constant.
- `read_homeHolderMatchesTheDeviceDefaultHome` — the home holder equals the device's
  actual default launcher (`com.google.android.apps.nexuslauncher`), cross-checked
  against `adb shell cmd role get-role-holders`.

Artifacts in `.github/issue-evidence/9967-androidtest-*`: gradle HTML report, JUnit XML,
`TestRunner` logcat, the live role-holder dump, device screenshot, and a screenrecord.

## Scope (honest)

This is the **foundational slice** of the #9967 work-order: it makes the previously
untestable native Kotlin testable and proves it on hardware, establishing the
standalone-instrumentation pattern. It does **not** yet cover the issue's other asks —
deterministic gradle wiring so all 9 native plugins compile into the launcher APK
(gap #1), the full native-plugin × view matrix across the remaining plugins, and the
launcher-as-home behavior tests. Those build directly on this pattern and are tracked
as the remaining #9967 surface.

b:Teb4,## What

Lands the first **"Needs CI infra"** deliverable spun out of #9476 (tracked by #10333): run the MiniWoB++ benchmark suite against a **real Chromium engine** instead of JSDOM web mode.

The benchmark adapter was already engine-agnostic (`BrowserCommandExecutor`), so this is a **new executor + a gated lane — not a rewrite**. `report.engine` flips from `"jsdom-web"` to `"chromium"` and every action runs through a real browser process, mirroring how plugin-computeruse's OSWorld `*.real.test.ts` lanes drive a real OS.

## How

- **`src/benchmark/chromium-executor.ts`** — `createChromiumBenchmarkExecutor` (puppeteer-core, already a dep), `launchChromiumBenchmarkBrowser` (one browser per suite, fresh page per episode), `resolveChromiumExecutablePath` (env → playwright-core → system Chrome; `null` → lane self-skips). Command semantics mirror `browser-workspace-web.ts` **exactly** (type appends, fill replaces, check sets `checked`, get value/checked/text/count/url/title, snapshot, network-route via request interception with a hard external-network seal), so the **identical oracle sequences solve every task on both engines** — the engine-parity proof.
- **`src/benchmark/__tests__/miniwob-chromium.real.test.ts`** — gated `.real.test.ts` lane: oracle solves every task on every seed (reward 1), noop scores 0, on real Chromium.
- **`vitest.real.config.ts`** — opts the `.real.test.ts` lane back in (the root config excludes `**/*.real.test.ts`) while keeping the `@elizaos/*` source aliases. The "(or a dedicated config)" the issue called for.
- **`.github/workflows/browser-real-bench.yml`** — CI gate: `bunx playwright install --with-deps chromium`, run the lane + upload artifacts (nightly + dispatch + on benchmark changes), alongside the other heavy real lanes.
- **`scripts/run-miniwob-chromium-benchmark.mjs`** + `bench:miniwob:chromium` / `test:real-chromium` scripts.
- `BrowserWorkspaceMode` gains `"chromium"` (emitted only by this executor, never by `executeBrowserWorkspaceCommand`) so results carry an accurate engine tag.

## Evidence (real Chromium — `Google Chrome for Testing` via playwright)

```
$ bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-browser test:real-chromium
 ✓ src/benchmark/__tests__/miniwob-chromium.real.test.ts (2 tests) 84.5s
   ✓ oracle policy solves every task on every seed (reward 1) through real Chromium
   ✓ noop baseline scores 0 on real Chromium — reward discriminates, never auto-passes
 Test Files  1 passed (1)   Tests  2 passed (2)
```

Committed run artifacts in `.github/issue-evidence/10333-browser-real-chromium/`:

| Artifact | engine | policy | solved |
|----------|--------|--------|--------|
| `miniwob-chromium-oracle-run.json` | chromium | oracle | **18 / 18** (100%) |
| `miniwob-chromium-noop-run.json`   | chromium | noop   | **0 / 12** |

Reproduce:
```bash
bunx playwright install --with-deps chromium
bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-browser test:real-chromium
bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-browser bench:miniwob:chromium --policy oracle --seeds 3
```

Without a Chromium binary the lane self-skips and the script is a clean no-op, so the un-gated path stays green.

## Scope / still deferred on #10333

This PR delivers the **real-Chromium engine lane + its CI gate**. The other two #10333 items — an external-dataset benchmark (Mind2Web/WebArena) and a web-element grounding benchmark (ScreenSpot-Web/VisualWebBench) — need multi-GB external datasets / dockerized site environments and remain genuinely CI-infra-gated. Both extend through the same `BrowserCommandExecutor` seam this PR exercises.

Closes part of #10333 (real-Chromium lane + CI gate); leaves the issue open for the two dataset lanes.

c:Tede,## What

Closes the one Definition-of-Done deliverable [verified missing on `develop`](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/issues/9476#issuecomment-4830133507) for #9476:

> **≥1 benchmark wired through `plugin-browser`** (mirror `plugin-computeruse/src/osworld/adapter.ts`), with a committed run artifact.

The other three DoD items already landed (CI-asserted parity matrix, JSDOM real-code lane, typed error contract). The web benchmarks (Mind2Web, WebShop, VisualWebBench) all **bypass** plugin-browser via the inference layer — no benchmark drove the BROWSER command surface end-to-end. This adds one that does.

## How

A **MiniWoB++-style** web-interaction benchmark (the canonical web-agent benchmark) under `plugins/plugin-browser/src/benchmark/`, the plugin-browser analog of `plugin-computeruse/src/osworld/`:

- **`adapter.ts`** — `BrowserBenchmarkAdapter` with the same observation/action/step seam as `OSWorldAdapter`, driving an engine-agnostic `BrowserCommandExecutor`. The workspace executor binds the **real** `executeBrowserWorkspaceCommand` router (the same mock-free path the `browser-workspace-web-real-code` lane uses) — no mock service stands in for the browser.
- **`tasks.ts`** — 6 tasks (`click-button`, `click-link`, `enter-text`, `enter-text-dynamic`, `click-checkboxes`, `multistep-purchase`). Pure markup, **no page scripts** (web mode hard-blocks script exec, GHSA-mhhr-9ph9-64j7); deterministic per seed; reward computed from **observable DOM state** read back through real BROWSER `get` commands (`value`/`checked`/`url`/`title`).
- **`policy.ts`** — `OraclePolicy` (deterministic solver) + `NoopPolicy`/`WrongPolicy` negative baselines.
- **`runner.ts`** — `runBenchmarkSuite` drives every task×seed episode, scores it, aggregates.
- **`__tests__/miniwob-adapter.test.ts`** — CI-asserted real-code lane (runs in the default `vitest run`).
- **`scripts/run-miniwob-benchmark.mjs`** + `bench:miniwob` — runnable harness that writes a JSON run report.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)10:T15c9,Closes #9952. **Onboarding now happens inside the SAME `ContinuousChatOverlay` used everywhere** — the chat is the first surface, the separate onboarding UI is deleted. Net **+2,144 / −23,475** (≈ −21k LOC).

Built on the chat-centric design @lalalune/the fleet drafted in **#10268** (`IN_CHAT_ONBOARDING_DESIGN.md`) — that PR is **design-only and still unmerged** (its design doc is **not** on develop). This branch is the full implementation + the real-shell e2e it called for. (Coordinating: this is a separate branch; reconcile with #10268 as you prefer.)

## What
- **Land in chat.** `firstRunComplete=false` is now shell-paintable: a fresh profile paints the homescreen + auto-opened `ContinuousChatOverlay` — no full-screen `StartupFirstRunBackground`/`FirstRunChat` gate.
- **Seed onboarding into the live transcript.** A headless conductor (`use-first-run-conductor.ts`) pushes synthetic assistant messages: greeting → `[CHOICE:first-run id=runtime]` (Cloud/Local/Other) → Cloud OAuth via the message `secretRequest` field → `[CHOICE:first-run id=provider]` (role-correct default) → `[CHOICE:first-run id=tutorial]` (Take tutorial / Skip). Renders via the existing `InlineWidgetText` + `SensitiveRequestBlock` — **no new widget components**.
- **Display-only widgets; logic in a use case.** First-run choice values (`__first_run__:` sentinel) are intercepted in `sendActionMessage` and routed to `first-run-finish.ts` (the surviving `finishLocal`/`finishCloud`/`selectOrProvision`/`submitFirstRun` + `first-run-config` defaults). `POST /api/first-run` fires **exactly once** (module-guarded). Tutorial pick → existing `tutorial-controller.startTutorial`.
- **Deleted** (≈23k lines): `CompactOnboarding`, `use-first-run-controller` (1,454-LOC step machine) + 3 tests, `AgentPicker`, `onboarding-intent`, the dedicated first-run **voice stack** (`first-run-tts-route` + unwired from app-core `server.ts`, `voice-readiness`, `onboarding-voice-lines`, `synthesizeFirstRunSpeech`), the dead `.eliza-onboarding-overlay-shell` CSS, and the dead `CompactOnboarding` e2e harness.

Desktop + **mobile**.

## Honest follow-up (tracked, not silently dropped)
- **Device remote-connect-at-URL onboarding (Android/iOS) — regressed, tracked in #10322.** The conductor offers Cloud/Local/Other; `runtime:other` runs the **local** backend and there is **no in-chat "connect to a remote host agent at a URL" step** (`finishRemote` exists in `first-run-finish.ts` but is unreachable — the in-chat choice channel only routes discrete button values, with no text/URL capture seam). Wiring it is a genuine **product/design decision** (a `runtime:other` URL sub-flow vs. a deep-link vs. a Settings entry), so rather than guess: the Android lane spec (`onboarding-to-home.android.spec.ts`) is `test.skip(true, …)` with the issue link, and the iOS smoke (`runIosOnboardingSmokeIfRequested` + `ios-onboarding-smoke.mjs`) now self-skips cleanly (the `NEEDS-HUMAN-REDESIGN` markers are removed). Both quarantines link **#10322**, which captures the redesign + how to un-skip.

16:T1f46,Closes #10553

## Problem

`PayoutProcessorService.processBatch` was supposed to recover redemptions whose worker died mid-payout, but the recovery was **dead code**. It selected:

```
status = 'approved' AND (processing_started_at IS NULL OR processing_started_at < now - LOCK_TIMEOUT)
```

A locked row has `status = 'processing'`, and `markFailed(retryable)` always resets `processing_started_at` to `NULL` when returning a row to `approved`. So **no `approved` row ever has a non-NULL `processing_started_at`** — the `lt(processing_started_at, …)` branch is unreachable. A redemption whose worker was evicted mid-payout stayed in `status = 'processing'` **forever**: funds locked in `total_pending`, never retried, contradicting the file's documented "automatic retry up to MAX_RETRY_ATTEMPTS." The same dead pattern was duplicated in the unused repo method `getApprovedForProcessing`. Separately, the per-redemption loop had **no try/catch**, so one RPC throw aborted the whole batch.

The cron (`*/5 /api/cron/process-redemptions`, CRON_SECRET-gated) is live.

## Why a naive fix double-pays

You cannot just re-approve stuck `processing` rows. If the worker died **after** broadcasting the transfer but **before** marking completed, the tokens are already on their way — re-approving re-broadcasts and **double-pays**. Recovery must be able to tell "never broadcast" from "broadcast, awaiting confirmation."

## Fix

### 1. Record the broadcast hash *at broadcast time* — the load-bearing invariant
New nullable column **`broadcast_tx_hash`** (migration `0155`, additive `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`, documented rollback). It is written the **instant** the tx is broadcast — the moment `writeContract` returns, **before** `waitForTransactionReceipt`. It is distinct from `tx_hash`, which is only written on confirmed completion. This lets recovery prove on-chain state:

- `broadcast_tx_hash IS NULL` → the payout provably never left our process → **safe to retry**.
- `broadcast_tx_hash IS NOT NULL` → a tx may be in flight → **must reconcile on-chain, never re-broadcast**.

The **Solana** path is refactored from `sendAndConfirmTransaction` to `getLatestBlockhash` → sign → `sendRawTransaction` (capture signature) → **record** → `confirmTransaction`, so it records the signature pre-confirmation too. The invariant is therefore **uniform across EVM and Solana**.

### 2. Separate, provably-safe recovery selector (`recoverStaleProcessing`)
Runs at the top of `processBatch` over stale `processing` rows (`processing_started_at < now − LOCK_TIMEOUT`):

| Case | Condition | Action |
|---|---|---|
| Provably safe, retries left | `broadcast_tx_hash IS NULL AND retry < MAX` | Re-approve (clears lock, `retry_count + 1`); retried in the same batch |
| Provably safe, retries exhausted | `broadcast_tx_hash IS NULL AND retry >= MAX` | Mark `failed` (manual intervention) |
| **Broadcast, unconfirmed** | `broadcast_tx_hash IS NOT NULL` | **Left in `processing`**, raise a `high` ops alert for on-chain reconciliation — **never re-approved** |

Recovery **never auto re-broadcasts a payout that already broadcast**. `MAX_RETRY_ATTEMPTS` semantics preserved (recovery increments `retry_count` and is gated by it). Fresh locks (within the timeout) are untouched, so a live worker is never robbed of its row.

### 3. Per-redemption try/catch (`handleProcessingThrow`)
A throw no longer aborts the batch. The handler re-reads the row and is **broadcast-aware**:
- broadcast hash present → leave in `processing` (record the reason), **no auto-retry** (would double-pay);
- no broadcast hash → safe retryable failure (`markFailed(retryable)`).

`markFailed(retryable)` now also clears `broadcast_tx_hash`, so each retry starts from a clean never-broadcast state (it only reaches there when nothing transferred: pre-broadcast failure, EVM revert, or atomic Solana failure).

### 4. Dead-code removal
Removed the unused `getApprovedForProcessing` repo method (the duplicated dead stale-lock pattern) and now-unused imports.

## Safety invariant (why this can't double-pay)

> Recovery and the throw-handler **re-broadcast only when `broadcast_tx_hash IS NULL`**, and the broadcast hash is persisted **before** the confirmation wait on every network. A row that ever broadcast a transaction therefore carries a non-NULL hash (modulo a single-DB-write residual window, see below) and is routed to on-chain reconciliation, never re-approved.

## Evidence

Real-DB test suite (`payout-stale-lock-recovery.test.ts`) driving the **real** `processBatch` against in-process PGlite, stubbing only the chain clients (viem) and RPC/env helpers — the selectors, lock, recovery SQL, broadcast persistence, and try/catch all run for real:

- **(a)** stale `processing`, no broadcast hash → recovered, re-approved, paid out (`writeContract` called once, `retry_count = 1`).
- **(b)** stale `processing`, **with** broadcast hash → **not** re-approved, status unchanged, **`writeContract` never called** (no double-pay), ops alert raised.
- **(c)** a throw in one redemption doesn't abort the batch — the other redemption still pays out.
- **(d)** the broadcast hash is persisted **before** confirmation: when `waitForTransactionReceipt` throws, `broadcast_tx_hash` is set, `tx_hash` is NULL, status stays `processing` (no re-approve).
- **(e)** a fresh lock (within `LOCK_TIMEOUT`) is left for the live worker.
- **(f)** a provably-safe, retry-exhausted row is failed for manual intervention.

```
bun test src/lib/services/__tests__/payout-stale-lock-recovery.test.ts   → 6 pass / 0 fail
bun run --cwd packages/cloud/shared typecheck                            → no errors in changed files
biome check (changed files)                                             → clean
```
Migration `0155` validated on PGlite (applies, column present, idempotent re-apply); journal contiguous through idx 153. Existing `payout-status-resilience` / `payout-networks` suites still green.

## Deferred (justified)

Full **automated** on-chain reconciliation of broadcast-but-unconfirmed rows (look up the receipt by `broadcast_tx_hash`, then settle the ledger idempotently) is intentionally **out of this slice**. Those rows are made **safe** (never auto re-broadcast) and **visible** (left in `processing` + `high` ops alert), exactly as the issue endorses. Automated reconciliation needs idempotent ledger settlement against `markCompleted`/`redeemable_earnings` and is a separate, carefully-tested change.

There is a tiny **residual window** for the broadcast hash: the worker could die between the broadcast returning a hash and the single DB write that persists it (one statement, vs. the long confirmation wait that was the original exposure). This is the design the issue specifies ("the moment writeContract returns the hash, before waitForTransactionReceipt"). Closing it fully would require deterministic offline signing (`sendRawTransaction` of a pre-hashed signed tx) and is not in scope.

> ⚠️ Money path — please **maintainer-merge** (board #10561 protocol). Not self-merged.

1a:T1dd5,## Coding Cockpit — a mobile-first coding-agent view

Adds a developer-only `/cockpit` GUI view in `plugin-task-coordinator` that unifies the coding-agent workflow on one screen:

- **`CockpitModePicker`** (`@elizaos/ui`) — a per-session mode picker collapsing the sanctioned coding modes into one selector, with a Fast/Smart tier toggle for Eliza Cloud. Presentational; emits a `CockpitModeConfig`.
- **`CockpitNewSessionForm`** — a free-text goal + the picker; on submit it lowers the selected mode to an orchestrator create-task `providerPolicy` via `buildCockpitCreateTaskInput` and hands the parent a ready `CodingAgentCreateTaskInput`.
- **`CockpitView`** — composes the existing `OrchestratorRoomView` (the live task-room deck, already on develop) with the new-session form.
- **`CockpitRoute`** (`plugin-task-coordinator`) — the live container: polls `client.getOrchestratorRooms()` (the deck) and spawns via `client.createOrchestratorTask()`. Registered as a `developerOnly`, GUI-only `/cockpit` view through the standard manifest + view-bundle pattern.

All mode → `providerPolicy` lowering lives in pure functions in `packages/ui/src/components/cockpit/cockpit-modes.ts` (UI has no business logic). The three sanctioned modes:

1. **Eliza Cloud** — eliza-code on Cerebras, fast/smart tier (`gpt-oss-120b` / `zai-glm-4.7`)
2. **OpenCode** — Cerebras
3. **Claude / Codex** — via the TOS-safe subscription connector

The emitted `{preferredFramework, providerSource, model}` values match develop's `TaskProviderPolicy` and the create-task route's `asProviderPolicy` parser (`plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator/src/api/orchestrator-routes.ts`).

### My Runtimes (kept — it ships live)

Also includes **`MyRuntimesSection`** + **`MyRuntimesContainer`** + **`switchRuntimeNonDestructive`** — a local / cloud-dedicated / VPS-remote runtime switcher. The switch logic reuses develop primitives (`repointBaseUrl`, the `isTrustedRestoreApiBaseUrl` trust-gate, the agent-profile registry). It is **mounted live in Settings** (`registerSettingsSection`, system group) — not exported-but-unmounted dead code — so it ships as a working, tested part of this PR rather than being deferred. The non-destructive switch re-points the client in place (no `SWITCH_AGENT` dispatch, no draft-clear, no StartupScreen flash) and trust-gates remote runtimes (public URLs rejected; loopback/RFC1918/CGNAT/tailscale/same-origin allowed).

### What was stripped (and why)

1. **Dead "canonical" orchestrator service.** An earlier iteration had `plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator/src/services/cockpit-mode.ts` (~210 lines) + its test — a server-side lowering that was never exported or imported by any production code (only its own test), a dead duplicate of the UI's `cockpit-modes.ts`. **Deleted** (the UI mirror is the single source of truth). Orchestrator typecheck passes with zero dangling references.

2. **TOS-unsafe experimental picker options.** The `claude-experimental` / `codex-experimental` modes and the `experimentalEnabled` gate depended on a "stealth subscription inference" replay-proxy branch that has been **rejected and removed** from the repo. **Removed** the two experimental options, the `mode: "experimental"` config variant, the `exp` badge, and the `experimentalEnabled` prop that threaded through the picker → form → view (`CockpitRoute` never set it, so the gate was already permanently unreachable). Also removed the now-trivial `visibleCockpitModeOptions` helper and its barrel export. A regression guard in the picker test asserts the experimental testids stay absent.

1b:Td15,## What & why

Closes the stability proof-gap in #10197: the restart/watchdog code existed but nothing **acted** on memory pressure and no test proved crash-recovery. This PR makes the agent **restart cleanly before an OOM** and proves the recovery/soak/guard behavior with real, runnable tests + a one-command umbrella that induces the failures and scores them.

No second restart mechanism is introduced — everything funnels through the existing `requestRestart()` → `RESTART_EXIT_CODE=75` → `run-node.mjs` supervisor (CLI) or the host relaunch entrypoint (device). The watchdog is **opt-in** (`ELIZA_MEMORY_WATCHDOG`, default off) so production boot is byte-identical until enabled.

## Deliverables

| Area | File | Proof |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Memory watchdog** | `packages/agent/src/runtime/memory-watchdog.ts` (+ `eliza.ts` wiring) | RSS ≥ threshold for N samples → `[MemoryWatchdog]` warn + `requestRestart()`, never `process.exit`. DI'd; **12 unit tests** (threshold, sustained-trigger, transient-spike debounce, one-shot, env floors, timer-driven). |
| **Supervisor crash-recovery** | `packages/app-core/scripts/lib/restart-guard.mjs` (extracted from `run-node.mjs`, behavior-identical) | **5 guard unit tests** + **2 real-process integration tests** driving the actual `run-node.mjs` with a fake child (real exit-75) → relaunch + crash-loop abort. |
| **Soak / leak detector** | `packages/agent/src/runtime/memory-soak.ts` | `runSoak()` forces GC + reports heap slope; **2 tests** prove it flags a retained-allocation leak and reports a steady workload as flat. Closes the hole documented in `plugin-lifecycle-leaks.test.ts`. |
| **Umbrella + scoreboard** | `packages/app-core/scripts/stability-suite.mjs` (`stability:suite`) | One command **induces** crash→recovery + a leak workload, writes `10197-agent-stability-scoreboard.md`, exits non-zero on any failure. |
| **iOS watchdog parity** | `packages/app-core/platforms/ios/App/App/AgentWatchdog.swift` (+ `AppDelegate.swift`) | Liveness poll + bounded relaunch mirroring Android `ElizaAgentService`. |

- **Device-connected (Android/iOS) crash→recovery capture** — needs a physically-connected phone. Android watchdog already exists; iOS parity is added in this PR but the on-device capture (screenshot + recording + logcat/console) runs in `android-e2e.mjs --serial` / an iOS-device lane.
- **Cloud chainsaw kill/OOM → health restored** — needs a k8s cluster.
- **Real-LLM soak trajectory** — needs a live model key/budget; `runSoak` accepts a real message-loop `workload` for that lane.

1e:Tac3,## What this adds

Builds the **interactive spine** of the coding cockpit on top of the foundation shipped in #10419 (mode picker + new-session + My Runtimes). This is the part that makes it actually *replace the terminal workflow*:

- **Drill-in session pane** — tap a deck room → its live transcript (`useOrchestratorData` + `buildConversation` + `ConversationBlockView`) + the full `TaskInspector` control bar (pause/resume/fork/restart/validate/add-agent/stop-agent), lifted out of the workbench monolith so one room embeds cleanly.
- **Bubble → focused-agent binding** — adds `onSubmit` to `ViewChatBinding` + a short-circuit in `ContinuousChatOverlay.submitText`; while a session pane is open the one floating composer drives **that** task (`postOrchestratorTaskMessage`). The hybrid-driver UX.
- **Real-CLI terminal** — a Transcript⇄Terminal toggle in the pane mounting `PtyConsoleBase`/`PtyTerminalPane` over the task's PTY sessions (narrowed from `getCodingAgentStatus`).
- **Fast/Smart tier toggle** — for Eliza Cloud sessions: persists the `providerPolicy` + respawns on the other Cerebras model.
- **App-control intent** — "open the cockpit" / "my agents" matcher noun + `ORCHESTRATOR_CAPABILITIES` on the view.
- **Phone gating** — hides the local runtime on store/android-cloud builds.

## Also fixes a live bug on develop

`CockpitRoute` on develop (from #10419) still has the **B1 "spawn-nothing" bug** — "Start agent" created an idle task and spawned no agent. This PR fixes it (create **then** `addOrchestratorAgent` with the picked mode) + adds a regression test.

## ⚠️ Heads-up for @lalalune — experimental modes

#10419 removed the experimental TOS-unsafe Claude/Codex picker options. This branch **re-adds them, gated** (hidden unless a dev arms `experimentalEnabled`) — per nubs's call to keep them dormant. **Your veto** — happy to drop them to match develop if you'd rather they stay out.

## Evidence

- ~265 cockpit tests green (53 UI components + deck/binding/switch, 16 plugin route/pane/terminal, 196 matcher); typecheck clean (`packages/ui` + `plugin-task-coordinator` + `plugin-app-control`); biome clean; view bundle builds.
- Storybook screenshots of every cockpit story in the branch's planning notes.

## Honest deferrals (documented, not hidden)

- **Live interactive `/slash`** needs a runtime that registers `PTY_SERVICE` (node-pty); ACP sessions run `--no-terminal`, so the terminal ships as a read-mostly **watch** view.
- **No-restart in-place tier swap** would be net-new ACP `set_model` protocol; shipped persist-policy + respawn instead.
- **Live PR_EVIDENCE** (real-LLM trajectory / `audit:app` / on-device capture) is the remaining check — needs a booted agent with a model.

21:Tc7c,## What

Lands the first **"Needs CI infra"** item from #9476's deferred checklist
(tracked in #10333): a **real-Chromium engine lane** for the MiniWoB++
plugin-browser benchmark. The MiniWoB++ suite now runs end-to-end through an
actual browser, not just JSDOM web mode — the same engine-agnostic
`BrowserBenchmarkAdapter` seam, a new executor, a gated lane. Not a rewrite.

## How

- **New executor** — `src/benchmark/chromium-executor.ts`: a
  `BrowserCommandExecutor` (`engine: "chromium"`) that drives the same
  `BrowserWorkspaceCommand`s (`navigate`/`click`/`type`/`fill`/`check`/`press`/
  `get`/`snapshot`) against a real Chromium-family browser via **puppeteer-core**
  (already a plugin-browser dependency — same as the computeruse real lanes; no
  Chromium bundled). Each episode renders the task's routed HTML through request
  interception — no external network, exactly like the JSDOM lane's
  `network route`. The executable resolves from an env override → a
  Playwright-installed Chromium (the `bunx playwright install chromium` pattern)
  → system Chrome/Edge/Brave.
- **New gated lane** — `src/benchmark/__tests__/miniwob-chromium.real.test.ts`:
  excluded from the default unit config (root vitest excludes `*.real.test.ts`),
  run via `packages/test/vitest/real.config.ts`. Self-skips when no
  Chromium-family browser is resolvable, so it is a clean no-op where one is not
  installed. Asserts **both directions** on the real engine: the **oracle solves
  every task×seed** (reward 1, every step an `engine: "chromium"` command), and
  the **noop baseline fails** (reward 0) — so the reward is grounded in real
  rendered DOM, not hard-coded.
- **Scripts** — `test:real:chromium` (the gated lane) and
  `bench:miniwob:chromium` (screenshot + scorecard evidence capture).

## Evidence (real browser, on Windows)

`.github/issue-evidence/10333-browser-real-chromium/`:
- `scorecard.json` — oracle on a real Chromium: **6/6 tasks solved**, per-task
  real-command trajectory.
- Per-task **start / solved screenshots** of the real rendered pages — e.g.
  `05-click-checkboxes-solved.png` shows exactly the requested checkbox ("lemon")
  checked and the rest left unchecked: the reward criterion, visually confirmed
  in a real browser.

Locally: the gated lane passes (oracle solves every task×seed on the
Playwright-installed Chromium; noop fails). puppeteer-core's WebSocket CDP works
under Bun (unlike Playwright's fd-pipe on Windows).

## CI wiring (workflow-scope-gated — needs a maintainer)

I can't add the CI step from this environment (the token lacks GitHub's
`workflow` scope, which blocks `.github/workflows/*` over both git and the API).
A maintainer should add, alongside the CUA real lanes, a gated job that runs
`bunx playwright install --with-deps chromium` then
`bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-browser test:real:chromium` (exact step in a PR
comment). The lane is committed, runnable, and green locally.

## Still deferred on #10333

External-dataset benchmark (Mind2Web/WebArena) through plugin-browser, a
web-element grounding benchmark, and the CI gating itself — larger external
datasets / runner work; remain tracked on #10333.

25:Tede,## What & why

Closes the remaining **code** work on #9899. The pre-forward TTFT root cause is fixed and live (Tier 1 auth cache + Tier 2 optimistic billing). The issue + `packages/cloud/api/docs/inference-hot-path.md` named one remaining step: the **DB-backed pending-charge + settlement ledger** that closes the three at-scale residuals a KV-only backstop *cannot*. This implements it behind `INFERENCE_BILLING_LEDGER="db"` (default `""`/`kv` ⇒ **no behavior change** anywhere until ops flips it), matching the soak-then-cutover discipline the issue itself prescribed.

## Residuals closed (point-for-point)

1. **Hard concurrent-overdraw bound** — admission is ONE atomic `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` + `SUM(pending)` statement; a same-org burst serializes on the row lock and cannot collectively overdraw (was: KV soft bound via threshold only).
2. **True exactly-once settlement** — `request_id` PK + atomic `UPDATE … WHERE status='pending'` claim ⇒ inline settler and cron sweep can never both charge; the debit funnels through the single audited `creditsService.deductCredits`. No more KV single-flight lock (was: near-atomic get-then-delete ⇒ rare double-bill).
3. **Age-ordered sweep drain** — `ORDER BY enqueued_at LIMIT batch` loop over a partial index, no silent `maxKeys` cap.

Plus `uncollected` is now a first-class auditable row state; the org self-heals onto the synchronous-reserve path on a refused debit.

## Rollout (default OFF, no prod change)

`INFERENCE_BILLING_LEDGER` is `""` in every env block (still requires `INFERENCE_OPTIMISTIC_BILLING="true"`). The KV path is **byte-identical** when the flag is unset and stays as the rollback target. Cutover = ship migration `0153`, flip to `"db"` in staging, watch `[InferenceLedger] uncollected …` + sweep stats, then prod.

## Evidence (real path, no larp)

**90 pass / 0 fail** across the touched + related suites, every test driving the **real SQL against in-process PGlite** (same pattern as the audited `credits-deduct-guard.test.ts`):

|---|---|
| `inference-billing-ledger.test.ts` | 15 — admission (overdraw bound, threshold, unknown org, +Inf, idempotent, concurrency) · settle (actual, **exactly-once**, settle(0), uncollected) · sweep (stale/young, inline-then-sweep, age-ordered multi-batch) |

29:T9bf,Stacked on #10475 (base `fix/10469-secret-prompt-swap`). Completes the secret/PII swap layer end-to-end: **detection + verification + egress**.

## Detection — 16 validated classes (`pii-detectors.ts`)
Each is a regex + structural validator so false positives are rejected: `credit-card` (Luhn + brand), `iban` (mod-97), `ssn` (SSA rules), `ipv4` (octet range), `email`, `jwt`, `phone`, `mac-address`, `hex-secret`, and cloud/provider keys (aws/stripe/google/github/openai/slack, PEM private-key). `detectPii()` resolves overlaps (longest wins); per-class opt-out (`disabledKinds`) on top of per-value `exemptValues`.

## Security hardening — unforgeable session nonce
Placeholders are `__ELIZA_SECRET_<nonce>_<n>__`; restore + the exec-boundary assert are scoped to the session nonce, so a user/model can't forge a placeholder to hijack a real secret, and benign placeholder-shaped text isn't falsely failed. A this-session placeholder the model *fabricated* fails loud.

## Egress wired (the execution boundary) — gated, default-off, zero-cost when disabled
- **Turn-scoped session** on the AsyncLocalStorage trajectory context (spans ingress `useModel` → action exec). `useModel` mints it once per turn and reuses it so all calls share a nonce.
- **Single restore funnel**: `executePlannedToolCall` (every model-selected action — top-level/sub-planner/autonomy) restores real secrets into handler args just before `action.handler`; the model/transcripts/logs/trajectory keep placeholders. A fabricated placeholder **fails loud** — never sent to a real command/connector/endpoint.

## Tests — 131/131 green; `packages/core` typecheck + biome clean
Validity (21), **fuzz** (seeded: 4000 secret docs + 2000 unicode — round-trip/no-leak/deterministic/idempotent), **red-team** (10), **benchmark** (substitute+restore ~371 hz/2.7 ms @ 50 KB), **egress e2e** (3: real secret reaches handler; fabricated → fail loud; disabled → no-op), runtime integration + existing exec suites unchanged (28/28).

**3 real bugs the fuzz/red-team caught + fixed:** phone FP on bare digit runs; a credit-card regex that ate a leading stray digit, failed Luhn, and **leaked the card**; placeholder forgery/collision.

Evidence: `.github/issue-evidence/10469-pii-detectors-and-tests.md`. Remaining: a live-model trajectory (needs a provider key, unset here) — the path is proven deterministically with a real `SecretSwapSession`.

## What this does

1. **New detector — literal-set duplicates (class 4).** `type-duplication-audit.mjs` now clusters string-literal unions, `enum`s, and `as const` maps by their **value set** across packages — even under different names. It surfaces 211 families today (e.g. `LogLevel` = `debug|error|info|warn` in 6 packages; `RoleName`/`RequiredRole`/… = `ADMIN|GUEST|OWNER|USER` in 6 packages; the cloud-api status unions). 2. **Consolidated family — connector-setup `SetupState`.** `SetupState` / `SetupStatusResponse` / `SetupErrorResponse` / `SETUP_ERROR_CODES` / `buildSetupError` / `setupPath` were declared verbatim in `@elizaos/app-core/api/setup-contract.ts` and **re-mirrored in 7 connector setup-routes + 3 data-routes files**. They now live once in `packages/core/src/types/connector-setup.ts` — the inward package every connector already depends on (and which already hosts `Route`/`RouteRequest`/`RouteResponse`). `app-core` re-exports them (import path unchanged); the per-connector `setupError` helpers collapse into the shared `buildSetupError`. `plugin-telegram` keeps a *specialized* `SetupStatusResponse` built on the shared `SetupState` (legitimate refinement). The cheap self-test is wired into `quality.yml` / `quality-fork.yml`; the full scan stays off the hot path.

Real macOS/Windows Electrobun, iOS/Android device live captures, openWakeWord real-head, Stage-B STT evaluation, and full hardware-backed screen/audio artifacts remain required for #9958 overall.2c:T1141,## #9142 Part 2 — single-frame visual-glitch e2e harness

Part 1 (the *static* invisible drag-handle regression) is already fixed and guarded. This adds the missing **Part 2**: a harness that catches **transient** glitches — a wrong state that shows for **one frame** mid-animation, which a post-settle screenshot can never see. The issue's reopen comment enumerates exactly this gap ("No frame-sequence glitch harness … no consecutive-frame pixelmatch … no deliberately-broken canary").

### What it does

`packages/ui/src/components/shell/__e2e__/run-chat-sheet-frame-glitch-e2e.mjs` (animations **ON**, no determinism shim):

1. esbuild-bundles the real `chat-sheet-fixture.tsx` (same approach as the existing `run-chat-sheet-e2e.mjs`), loads it in headless Chromium, and drives a **real pointer gesture** that opens the sheet from its collapsed pill.
2. Captures a dense **CDP screencast frame burst** across the spring transition (every painted frame — far denser than `page.screenshot()` polling).
3. **Single-frame flash detector** — `pixelmatch`es every consecutive frame pair. A monotonic animation diffs smoothly; a one-frame flash at frame *k* is an **outlier**: it differs hard from *both* neighbours while the neighbours agree with each other (`diff(k-1,k+1) ≪ diff(k-1,k), diff(k,k+1)`). Those *k* are flagged.
4. **"Two pills" crossfade guard** — every rAF, samples the **effective** opacity (product up the DOM tree) of the pill bar (`chat-pill`) and grabber bar (`chat-sheet-grabber`). The component crossfades them with **no overlap** (`pillOpacity` over openProgress [0,0.55], `grabberOpacity` over [0.55,0.95]); the harness asserts they're never both visible at once — the exact "two bars stranded on screen" bug.
5. `--canary` injects a one-frame wrong state into **both** detectors and asserts each **FIRES**, proving the harness is not a no-op.

Evidence (frame burst, per-frame diff overlay, opacity trace, summary, console log) → `.github/issue-evidence/9142-frame-glitch/`.

### Evidence

- **Detector math, validated on synthetic frames** (standalone, no browser): a smooth slide → **0 flashes** (no false positives); an injected one-frame flash → **flagged at exactly that frame**; a monotonic scene change that *stays* → **correctly NOT flagged** (distinguishes a transient flash from a real state change).
- **Live run (Windows 11, headless Chromium under node)** — real round-trip (flick down to the pill, flick up to open) that crosses the crossfade both ways:
  - **34 frames + 142 opacity samples** captured across the spring transition.
  - **crossfade exercised**: pill swept to 1.00, grabber swept to 1.00 (full anti-phase morph).
  - **0 single-frame flashes**; **worst pill+grabber overlap = 0.000** → the strict crossfade holds on `develop` (the "two pills" invariant is upheld). Stable across 3 consecutive runs.
  - **`--canary`: both detectors FIRE** on the injected one-frame wrong state — proving the harness is not a no-op.
  - Evidence committed: `frame-*.png` (the burst), `diff-overlay-peak-*.png` (pixelmatch per-frame diffs), `summary.json` (per-state trace), `console.log`. The open-sheet frame and the peak diff overlay were reviewed by hand (brand-faithful render; the peak diff is the legitimate thread-content slide, correctly not flagged).
  - **Robustness**: the harness hides the composer's blinking caret and gates flash detection to the active transition window — a first naive run flagged a caret-blink false positive in the static settle tail; both guards eliminate it without masking real (large-diff, mid-animation) glitches.

### CI

Add this step to `.github/workflows/ui-e2e-gate.yml` (it already gates the sibling chat-sheet runners; mirrors `ui-story-gate.yml`). The step is included as a diff below rather than in the commit because this branch is pushed without `workflow` token scope:

```yaml
      - name: Chat sheet frame-glitch e2e (transient visual glitches)
        run: bun run --cwd packages/ui test:chat-sheet-frame-glitch-e2e -- --canary
```
and add `packages/ui/src/components/shell/__e2e__/output-frame-glitch` + `.github/issue-evidence/9142-frame-glitch` to the artifact upload paths.

Closes the Part-2 close-criteria (animations-ON frame burst, consecutive-frame pixelmatch, two-pills guard, canary). Runs under **node** (Playwright's CDP screencast pipe is unavailable under bun).

2d:Ta36,## What

Closes the **"real-Chromium engine lane"** — the first of #10333's deferred items (the secondary gap from #9476's Definition of Done). The #9476 benchmark drives the MiniWoB++ suite through plugin-browser's JSDOM web-mode router; this drives the **same** suite + adapter + oracle against a **real Chromium engine** (Microsoft Edge via puppeteer-core).

## How

The benchmark adapter is engine-agnostic (`BrowserCommandExecutor`), so **no task/adapter/runner code changed** — only a new executor:

- **`src/benchmark/chromium-executor.ts`** — `createChromiumBenchmarkExecutor()` returns a `BrowserCommandExecutor` (`engine: "chromium"`) that maps each BROWSER command (`network route` / `navigate` / `click` / `type` / `fill` / `check` / `get` / `snapshot`) to a puppeteer page action, and serves the routed task HTML via puppeteer **request interception** — the real-browser analog of web mode's `network route` interceptor (no external network, deterministic). `resolveChromiumExecutable()` auto-detects Edge/Chrome (or `PUPPETEER_EXECUTABLE_PATH`).
- **`__tests__/miniwob-chromium.real.test.ts`** — a gated real-engine lane: skips cleanly when no Chromium is present, and is `*.real.test.ts` so the default `vitest run` excludes it. Oracle solves all 6 tasks (reward 1) through real Chromium, asserts every executed step ran with `commandResult.mode === "chromium"`, and a noop policy scores 0 (reward discriminates on the real engine too). Captures a per-task screenshot from the real browser.
- **`bench:miniwob-chromium`** runner + report artifact.

## Evidence — `.github/issue-evidence/9476-browser-benchmark/chromium/`

Real Edge on a Windows 11 host:

- `miniwob-chromium-run.json` — **18/18 solved (100%)**, `engine: "chromium"`, oracle, 3 seeds × 6 tasks.
- 6 real-browser screenshots, e.g.:
  - `task-click-button.png` → the routed **WOB SUCCESS** page (`episode reward = 1`) after the oracle clicked the labelled button,
  - `task-enter-text.png` → the field holding exactly the requested token (`Enter "Tobias" …` → `Tobias`) typed by puppeteer.

Run:
```bash
bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-browser bench:miniwob-chromium
bunx vitest run plugins/plugin-browser/src/benchmark/__tests__/miniwob-chromium.real.test.ts --config packages/test/vitest/real.config.ts
```

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)2f:Ta1b,## Summary

Follow-up to #10211 (merged), which landed the `migrate-agent` tool with only the flat-home reader. This PR makes the OpenClaw reader **version-robust** across the real agent-home formats and adds coverage, plus a few correctness hardenings surfaced in review.

## What migrate-agent does

`elizaos migrate-agent` reads a file-based OCPlatform ("moltbot") agent home (SOUL/IDENTITY/AGENTS/USER/TOOLS + memory/) and produces either:
- a portable encrypted `.eliza-agent` archive (`--out`, consumed by `importAgent`), or
- sovereign-local artifacts: character JSON + memories JSONL (`--emit-*`).

It is **self-contained**: node built-ins + local type mirrors only, no agent-runtime dependency.

## Version-robustness (this PR)

The reader now tolerates every OC home shape:
- **FLAT** (`.moltbot`): `<home>/SOUL.md`, `IDENTITY.md`, `MEMORY.md`, `memory/*.md`
- **NESTED**: `<home>/workspace/SOUL.md` (probes `<home>` then `workspace` / `workspace.default`). A nested home previously migrated to an empty character silently.
- **LEAN** (`.hermes`): SOUL + AGENTS only, persona-named `*-awareness.md` / `*-thoughts.md`
- **LEGACY**: curated memory at lowercase `memory.md`, not just `MEMORY.md`
- **SQLITE** (builder): detect + warn, best-effort read of `chunks.text` via the optional `node:sqlite` builtin (no heavy dep). Awareness recovered from sqlite is promoted to CURRENT. Partial multi-store failures are surfaced, not hidden behind the success message.

## Firewall

`USER.md` (personal context about the human) is **excluded by default**. `--no-firewall` opts in for owner-local migrations only.

## Other hardening

- Name derivation only treats a **leading** SOUL H1 as the persona title, so a SOUL that opens with prose and has a later `# Voice` section falls back to the agent-id slug instead of being misnamed.
- `--json` mode sets quiet **before** any validation that can fail, keeping stdout machine-parseable on early errors.
- Cross-tier memory dedup + truncation reporting in `memory-tiering`.
- Removed two unused workspace deps (`@elizaos/core`, `@elizaos/agent`) that were never in `bun.lock` and would fail a frozen install.
- No em-dashes in migrate source or design doc.

## Tests

**21 tests** (single self-contained `migrate.test.ts`): flat/nested/lean/legacy/sqlite classification incl. sqlite awareness recovery, leading-SOUL-heading name guard, full `.eliza-agent` crypto round-trip + referential integrity, USER.md firewall, `--json` stdout purity. `tsgo` clean on `src/migrate`. Codex-reviewed clean.

30:T675,Two orchestrator trajectory fixes from a live-trace debug session. Both unit-tested (40 tests). See the known-gap note below.

## Bug A: duplicate reply delivery
The same logical assistant reply was delivered twice. Adds a delivery-dedupe guard (packages/agent/src/api/delivery-dedupe.ts) that suppresses a re-delivery of the same (roomId + normalized text) within a short window across fan-out sinks (client_chat send handler + autonomy relay). 2000ms window (cross-path fan-out lands within ms). Attachments discriminate distinct media sends. +26 tests.

## Bug B: incidental narration URLs triggering dead-URL retry
On task_complete the verifier probed EVERY URL in the sub-agent's narration; for a local-folder build with no URL claim, an incidental routed-domain URL surfaced by codex's exploration (e.g. it greps skill code) got probed, failed, and re-dispatched as 'failed verification' -> the user saw 'something glitched, give me another go' on a successful build. Now only verify URLs when the task actually requested a reachable/provided artifact; taskRequestsProvidedUrl excludes input-url verbs (get/need/want, as in 'get the URL X and summarize it'). +14 tests.

## KNOWN GAP (honest)
Live testing showed the dedupe guards the API-layer fan-out but NOT the core basic-capabilities send path (packages/core/src/features/basic-capabilities/index.ts ~line 998), which also emits 'Message sent' and can still double on some turns. A follow-up should route that path through the same dedupe. The two fixes here are correct + tested in isolation; full live-clean trajectory needs that one more hook.

## What changed

### 1 — `build:core`: 930-char `--filter` wall → declarative metadata + drift guard (the issue's headline)
`build:core` is load-bearing (6 CI/deploy workflows + `test:server`/`test:client`/`test:plugins` all run it first) but was a hand-curated wall of 27 `--filter=@elizaos/…` flags that a new dependency had to be appended to by hand and a renamed package would rot silently.

- **Before:** `node packages/scripts/run-turbo.mjs run build --filter=@elizaos/contracts --filter=… (×27)` — 930 chars.
- **After:** `node packages/scripts/build-core.mjs` — the 27 leaf packages live in `build-core-packages.mjs` (grouped, one per line, with rationale), and the driver expands them into the **byte-identical** Turbo invocation. Zero behaviour change.
- New `__tests__/build-core.test.ts` drift guard: every package resolves to a real workspace package, no dupes, root script delegates with **no** re-inlined `--filter`. Set-equality with the old list and Turbo's exact 27-package scope resolution verified.

### 2 — Script inventory now covers `packages/app` (the second dense surface)
`audit-scripts-inventory.mjs` classified only `packages/scripts/*.mjs` + root scripts. It now also classifies all **80** `packages/app/package.json` scripts by reachability — modelling Turbo task fan-out, `--cwd packages/app <name>`, `working-directory: packages/app` CI step blocks, app→app `bun run <name>`, and npm pre/post lifecycle pairs. (23 CI-reachable, 4 verify/build, 53 no-automated-caller dev/maintainer entrypoints — the report says "orphan ≠ delete".)

### 4b — Shared `plugin-build.ts` driver self-test
57 plugin `build.ts` files delegate to `buildPlugin`, which had **no** test. `__tests__/plugin-build.test.ts` drives it against throwaway fixture packages and asserts the **real emitted `dist/` tree** across every path: clean/clean:false, `Bun.build` target, renames, `flatten`, `dtsProject`, `dtsShims`, `dtsCopies`, externals auto-derivation, build-failure-throws, `dtsTolerant`. 9 tests, 95.8% line cov; +2 for `externalsFromPackageJson`.

### 4a — Root script count: 204 (peak) → 203 (#10361) → **201**
Removed two proven byte-dup aliases (the merged inventory's "safe to remove first"): `dev:web:ui` (→ `dev`, text refs redirected) and `dev:cloud:full` (→ `dev:cloud`). **Kept** `test:cloud:playwright` (re-confirmed it backs the `scenario-pr-workflow` guard) and `test:cloud:full` (referenced by `check-live-test-artifact-coverage.mjs`).

### 3 — `run-all-tests.mjs` consolidation: deferred (documented)
Left untouched on purpose — a 1080-LOC orchestrator driving every test lane; rewriting risks destabilising `verify` for a lower-value payoff. Rationale in the evidence doc.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)35:Tc69,> ⚠️ **Draft pending device/visual review** — code-complete and compile/type/test/lint/web-build verified, but the on-device/visual pass (iOS sim / Android emu / Electrobun, `audit:app` verdicts) is the remaining with-owner step. ## What

Mounts the **Eliza Cloud Applications dashboard as a native view inside the Eliza app** (shaw's "dashboard as a view in Eliza"), the locked architecture: **mount the existing components, not the website** — no iframe, no `__ELIZA_WEB_SHELL__` gate-flip, no rebuild. Two parts:

### 1. Native-aware cloud api-client (the one hard blocker)
`packages/ui/src/cloud/lib/api-client.ts` was same-origin-only (threw `CROSS_ORIGIN_API_URL`), so on native every `/api/v1/apps*` call hit the local agent, not Cloud. Now native/Electrobun resolves the base to `api.elizacloud.ai` via `CapacitorHttp` + the existing Steward Bearer — **strictly gated** to native runtime **and** the single allowlisted host. The **web path is byte-identical** (same-origin + still throws on cross-origin), with a regression test asserting that.

### 2. The mount (`NativeAppsStudio`)
Wraps the existing `ApplicationsPage` + `ApplicationDetailPage` in a **`MemoryRouter`** + the shared cloud `QueryClient`/i18n + a **native Steward auth context** built from the stored JWT (`STEWARD_TOKEN_KEY`) — so `useRequireAuth()` resolves the signed-in user without the `@stwd/*` runtime; it tracks refresh/sign-out/401 and runs the native session refresh before first paint only when the JWT is near expiry. No `/login` route (native uses `launchStewardLogin`). Registered via `registerAppShellPage({ id:'cloud-apps', path:'/cloud-apps', label:'Apps', loader })`, **lazy** (emits its own chunk; tree-shake preserved), gated to non-web platforms. Per-tab pass routes the cross-domain/web-only sub-flows (connect, deploy-agent, view-earnings, external/support/verified-domain links) to the system browser on native via a new helper — **gated on the runtime detector so web is byte-intact**.

## Auth / safety
No backend change. Native already holds the same Steward JWT the dashboard reads; the Worker accepts cross-origin Bearer; native origins are first-party CORS. The `@stwd/react` cookie-mint is skipped on native (harmless). The #9947 embed-token model is untouched. Agent-made apps and GUI-made apps are the same org-scoped rows.

## Evidence
- `bun run --cwd packages/app build:web` → **✓ built, exit 0** (lazy `NativeAppsStudio`/`ApplicationsPage` chunks emitted, chunk-safety OK).
- `packages/ui` + `packages/app` typecheck clean; biome clean on all 9 touched files.
- Smoke test (jsdom render of the mount with a mocked api-client) 2/2; applications-domain + api-client suite 30/30.
- **Web-behavior risk: low** — studio + registration are native-only (runtime gate + lazy import); all native per-tab branches return `false` on web, leaving `<Link>`/anchor behavior identical.

Closes the remaining re-scoped delta of #10196 (the plugin-view "OS" missing live memory accounting + a real soak gate; the lifecycle/keep-alive/crash/telemetry primitives already landed via #10202/#10236/#10254).

### 1. Live heap accounting in the prune decision (`dbe13d1`)
The bounded module/view caches claimed memory-aware eviction but had **zero live-heap input** — prune sized only off the static `navigator.deviceMemory` hint, and the only memory-driven trigger was the `memorypressure` window event **Chromium never fires**. So "views evict under pressure" was unproven.

- `state/bounded-view-lru.ts`: `readJsHeap()` / `isHeapUnderPressure()` (0.8 of `jsHeapSizeLimit`) / `isCacheMemoryConstrained()`; **every** cap/TTL getter now routes through it, so live heap pressure drops all bounded caches to their low-memory tier. Centralized the remote-bundle thresholds here (they had silently **diverged** from retained-lazy — 6/2 local consts removed from `DynamicViewLoader`).
- `cache-telemetry.ts`: every `ModuleCacheTelemetryEvent` carries `jsHeapUsedSize` + a `heap-pressure` evict reason.
- new `state/heap-pressure-monitor.ts`: polls `performance.memory` while visible and dispatches the **real** `HEAP_PRESSURE_EVENT`; both module caches listen + force-evict idle entries.
- tests: `bounded-view-lru.heap.test.ts` (reader/pressure/cap-tiering/dispatch) + a `retained-lazy` heap-pressure eviction asserting cleanup ran and the ring carries the heap value. (`ViewTelemetryProfiler` is a no-op pass-through in prod, so the monitor — not the profiler — is the production-active source.)

### 2. The real `audit:views` soak (`703f7424`)
The only view-lifecycle soak on develop was a **synthetic-fixture** harness (fake alpha/beta views, no app server) — which #10196's "no mocks standing in for the thing under test" DoD disqualifies.

- `packages/app/test/ui-smoke/views-soak.spec.ts`: boots the real ui-smoke stack with the injected-session helpers, enumerates **every** view from `GET /api/views` (developer+preview on), opens + cycles each N=5 times (first pass = warmup, discarded), drains `__ELIZA_RENDER_TELEMETRY__` + `__ELIZA_MODULE_CACHE_TELEMETRY__` + `performance.memory`. Asserts: every view opens (crash isolation), no render-loop ERROR, bounded cacheSize, **render-telemetry plumbing is actually live** (so the render-loop check is not vacuous), and post-warmup heap does not leak (OR-gated slope+monotonic / growth).
- new `audit-views` playwright project (`--enable-precise-memory-info` + `--expose-gc`) + `audit:views` script.
- **First real run** (committed scorecard under `.github/issue-evidence/10196-views-state/`): 27 views, all open, 0 render-loops, post-warmup heap **flat** (growth 1.002, slope 0.45 MiB/pass, non-monotonic). Scorecard honestly notes the stub-backend limits (builtin views are static → module-cache/eviction + per-viewKind need `ELIZA_UI_SMOKE_LIVE_STACK=1`; heap-pressure eviction is unit-covered in #1).

### 3. Uniform crash telemetry + docs (`34d3639`)
`DynamicViewLoader` wrapped views in the **generic** `ErrorBoundary`, so a remote/plugin-view crash was contained but emitted **no** `markCrashed` / crash telemetry (unlike builtin views). Swapped to `ViewErrorBoundary`. Documented in `VIEW_LIFECYCLE.md`: why `useRenderGuard` (targeted component render-fn probes) and `ViewTelemetryProfiler` (systemic per-view commit rate) intentionally coexist; the new live-heap eviction path; and the real `audit:views` gate.

Refs #9958.39:T1033,## What

#9963 was re-opened noting only ~2-3 of its 9 acceptance criteria were met. This lands the **non-infra-gated local slice** the re-open comment called for — the two pieces that are *not* gated by external infrastructure and were verifiably absent on `develop`:

1. **The issue's stated proof-of-done** — a real **backup → wipe → restore → identical** round-trip e2e (no such test existed).
2. **A per-component integrity manifest** — the maintainer's *"per-component sha256 with loud fail-on-mismatch"* (restore previously relied only on the whole-blob AES-GCM auth tag).

## How

**`packages/agent/src/services/agent-export.ts`**
- `buildExportManifest()` digests each exported collection (`entities`, `memories`, `components`, `rooms`, `participants`, `relationships`, `worlds`, `tasks`, `logs`, `media`) as `sha256` over its canonical JSON plus a row count, embedded in the (already-encrypted) payload at export.
- `verifyExportManifest()` re-derives every digest at import **before any DB write**; `importAgent` throws an `AgentExportError` **naming the offending collection** on mismatch. The GCM tag authenticates the ciphertext as a whole — this adds per-collection granularity and a loud, named failure for a structurally-inconsistent payload (truncated / hand-edited / partially merged).
- Additive + back-compat: an export with **no** manifest (older file) still imports — verification is skipped, never a hard failure on absence.
- `canonicalize()` — deterministic key-sorted, `undefined`-dropping JSON so a collection's digest is identical across the `export → gzip → encrypt → decrypt → gunzip → JSON.parse` round-trip.

**`packages/agent/src/services/agent-export.roundtrip.test.ts`** — 10 tests:
- The round-trip: populate a full agent graph (1 world, 2 rooms, 3 entities, 4 participants, 1 component, 3 memories across `messages`+`facts`, 1 relationship, 1 task) + a content-addressed media byte → `exportAgent` to a real encrypted `.eliza-agent` buffer → import into a **separate, empty** store → assert every collection count + content round-trips (ids are deliberately remapped; the restored agent gets a **new** id).
- Wrong-password rejection (nothing written).
- Integrity detector: matching manifest verifies; survives a JSON serialize→parse round-trip; a row added without updating the manifest is flagged on `memories`; a content edit with unchanged count is flagged on `entities`; an absent manifest is back-compat OK; `canonicalize` sorts keys + drops `undefined`.

The DB engine in the test is a faithful in-memory adapter implementing exactly the methods `extractAgentData`/`restoreAgentData` call — the **thing under test** is the export/restore + manifest logic, not the SQL engine.

## Evidence — `.github/issue-evidence/9963-backup-restore/`

- `roundtrip-report.json` — a real run: export format, encrypted byte size, the full per-collection **sha256 integrity manifest**, the **restore counts** (`entities:3, memories:3, rooms:2, participants:4, …`), restored-agent-id differs from source.
- `sample-backup.eliza-agent` — a real encrypted backup produced by `exportAgent` (`ELIZA_AGENT_V1`, PBKDF2-600k + AES-256-GCM + gzip).
- `roundtrip-test-output.txt` — the vitest run, **10/10 passing** on a Windows host.

Reproduce:
```bash
bun run --cwd packages/agent test -- src/services/agent-export.roundtrip.test.ts
```

## Out of this slice (and why) — tracked, not silently dropped

- **Settings backup/restore-with-verify UI + first-run-after-restore** — display-gated (the dev box's RDP desktop session is currently disconnected, so live UI capture isn't possible); the backend round-trip needs no display and is what's delivered here.
- **Real-LLM post-restore recall trajectory / video** — needs a live model + display; deferred.
- **LifeOps auto-backup scheduler**; **cloud parity (R2 dual-target, real-image restore)** — scheduler-/image-gated.
- **Vault** — `agent-export.ts` deliberately strips `secrets`; this PR does not change that threat-model decision.

3a:T9f9,Closes **#8895** and **#8898**. Both shipped their primitives (#9020) but left the **enforcement dead** — no production caller. This wires both into the one live completion gate, `autoVerifyCompletion`, as a single linear pipeline (per the master design).

## The pipeline (orchestrator-task-service.ts)
1. **Structural envelope gate (#8895)** — `parseCompletionEnvelope` runs **before** the LLM judge. Malformed → `envelope_invalid` + `envelopeCorrection` re-prompt (**no model spend**); absent → fall through unchanged (back-compat); valid → stamp `metadata.completionEnvelope` + prepend `summarizeEnvelope` to the judge evidence.
2. **Independent read-only verifier (#8898)** — for code-change tasks (`shouldRunIndependentVerify`, gated `ELIZA_ORCHESTRATOR_INDEPENDENT_VERIFY`, default on) a **separate read-only ACP session** re-runs tests/build; its execution verdict feeds `validateTask` under a distinct id `independent-acp-verifier`. inconclusive → stay `validating`; !passed → block + re-prompt; passed → fall through.
3. **Text judge** fallback unchanged.

## Read-only enforcement (#8898 AC4)
New `"verifier"` ApprovalPreset: read+search+**execute** allowed, edit/write/delete **denied** at the native transport (`isOperationApproved` → `writeTextFile` throws `PermissionDeniedError`). The existing `readonly`=`--deny-all` couldn't run the verifier's own tests. One `reEngageOrEscalate` helper + one `autoVerifyAttempts`/`MAX` cap govern all three failure paths.

## The 6 formerly-dead primitives now have real production callers
`parseCompletionEnvelope` (L1707), `envelopeCorrection` (L1714), `summarizeEnvelope` (L1738), `shouldRunIndependentVerify` (L1995), `runIndependentVerification` (L2005), `spawnReadOnlyVerifier`+`approvalPreset:'verifier'` (L2013/L2026).

## Tests
- `auto-goal-verify` (4 envelope-gate + 3 independent-verifier cases via fake-ACP harness) + `acp-native-transport.verifier` (read-only enforcement) + `independent-verifier` → **25 pass / 0 fail** (re-verified locally via vitest).
- Full plugin suite green except one **unrelated pre-existing** `smithers-task-runner` load-timeout flake (passes 8/8 in isolation; references no touched code). Typecheck 0 errors.

The literal `writeTextFile`-rejects assertion is covered deterministically by `isOperationApproved`/`approvesPermissionRequest` here and exercised end-to-end (real `PermissionDeniedError` → `deny.log`) by the gated multi-account live e2e.

Closes #8895, #8898.

3c:Tb72,Follow-up to **#10373**, which landed the renderer consumer + the shared `FUSED_WAKE_EVENT` / `emitFusedWake` / `bridgeDetectorToFusedWake` contract. What was still missing — and what the #10386 review explicitly called the *"genuinely-new, salvageable piece"* — is the **desktop producer**: nothing ran the native head on desktop and forwarded `onFusedWake` over `sendToWebview('voice:fusedWake')`, so the on-device path never actually drove the bar. ## Chain (all real)
```
desktop mic → DesktopMicSource → FusedWakeManager (electrobun main, bun:ffi)
  → real OpenWakeWordGgmlModel.scoreFrame (libwakeword) → threshold+streak+refractory
  → onWake(WakeFireInfo) ──bridgeDetectorToFusedWake──▶ {stage:'head-fired', confidence}
  → sendToWebview('voice:fusedWake', detail)
  → registerDesktopFusedWake → emitFusedWake() → 'eliza:fused-wake'   (#10373 consumer)
  → useWakeController → useWakeListenWindow.onOpen → bar active + startCapture('converse')
```

## What's added
- **`FusedWakeManager`** (electrobun main, mirrors `SwabbleManager`): runs the standalone libwakeword head in the desktop Bun process and forwards each fire via the **shared** `bridgeDetectorToFusedWake` — producer + consumer use the same `FusedWakeEventDetail`, no drift. Inert (`started:false`) when the model isn't staged → never forces mic access without the on-device assets.
- **`@elizaos/plugin-local-inference/voice-wake`**: a narrow barrel (no heavy-engine/llama import edge) so the lean electrobun main bundle isn't bloated.
- **`registerDesktopFusedWake`** (renderer): subscribes `voice:fusedWake` → `emitFusedWake`, sets `__ELIZA_FUSED_WAKE__`, arms `fusedWake:start` at boot (`main.tsx`). No-op off-desktop.
- Wiring: `native/index.ts`, `rpc-handlers`, `rpc-schema`, electrobun dep.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)3d:T110a,Closes #10322. Follow-up to #9952 / #10302 (the in-chat onboarding redesign that deleted the remote-connect-at-URL surface and quarantined the two device lanes).

## Problem

After #10302, the in-chat conductor only offers `runtime:cloud | local | other`. There is **no remote-connect-at-URL path**: `finishRemote` exists in `first-run-finish.ts` but is unreachable, the device deep link only pre-selected the runtime (the URL had to be typed into a now-deleted DOM field), and `Settings → Runtime → Remote` dead-ends by reloading into a conductor that can't capture a URL. The Android + iOS device lanes that drove the old flow were skipped.

## Approach — deep link + Settings, decoupled from the onboarding surface

Per the product decision, remote-connect becomes a first-class capability that does **not** depend on which onboarding surface is active, so it works today and survives future redesigns. Both entry points funnel through the existing, security-hardened `CONNECT_EVENT` path (`applyLaunchConnection` + `url-trust-policy`; a bearer token is **never** accepted from an OS-delivered deep link).

- **Deep link carries the URL** — `parseFirstRunRemoteConnectDeepLink` recognizes `<scheme>://first-run/runtime/remote?api=<url>` (pure parser; `api`/`apiBase`/`url`/`host` aliases) and routes it through `connectFirstRunRemoteDeepLink` in `main.tsx` (trust-policy gated) → `CONNECT_EVENT { completeFirstRun: true }`. A bare `…/remote` link still falls through to the existing runtime pre-select.
- **Headless `adopt-remote-first-run.ts`** — the reachable, reusable successor to `finishRemote`. PROBES the remote's `GET /api/first-run/status` and only `POST`s `/api/first-run` when the host hasn't finished its own first-run — an improvement over the legacy unconditional POST, which could clobber an already-configured host. `retryStartup()` re-polls → lands on home.
- **`CONNECT_EVENT` handler** gains `completeFirstRun` (adopt the remote) + `skipConfirm` (trusted in-app callers); OS deep links keep the non-loopback confirmation prompt.
- **Settings → Runtime → Remote** now opens a "Connect a remote agent" URL + optional-token form (reusing `SettingsRow`/`Input`/`SettingsActionButton`) that dispatches the same `CONNECT_EVENT { completeFirstRun, skipConfirm }`.
- **Device lanes un-quarantined + rewritten to the real OS deep link** — Android fires an `adb` VIEW intent; the iOS smoke fires `simctl openurl` and the in-app verifier proves the post-connect home surface (no onboarding DOM driven, so the lanes survive the in-chat redesign). Removed the now-dead iOS DOM-driver helpers + the harness skip-handling.

## Verification

- **Unit — 36 passed**: `deep-link-entry.test.ts` (parser: aliases, decoding, negative cases) + `adopt-remote-first-run.test.ts` (`normalizeRemoteAgentUrl` + adopt: fresh→POST, already-complete→no-clobber, unreachable→still-POST, failure→propagates, token forwarded).
- **No regressions** — `packages/ui` first-run + shell suite 143 passed; `packages/app` deep-link suite 19 passed.
- **Typecheck** — `packages/ui` + `packages/app` clean for every touched file (only the pre-existing, unmodified `steward-wallet-providers.tsx` viem error remains, identical on `develop`).
- **Device e2e (real deep link)** runs in CI / on device hardware — iOS via `mobile-build-smoke.yml` (`ios-onboarding-smoke.mjs`), Android via `test:e2e:android:onboarding`; both produce `home-landing.png` + `onboarding-to-home.mp4` + `result.json`.
- Full evidence + aesthetic review: [`.github/issue-evidence/10322-device-remote-connect-onboarding.md`](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/shaw/frosty-elbakyan-54c6ef/.github/issue-evidence/10322-device-remote-connect-onboarding.md).
- **Trajectory / audio: N/A** — shell/first-run UX wiring, not an agent/model change (consistent with the #9952 evidence section).

3f:T5c2,## What & why

Adds the **app-blocker** to the #9967 on-device native-plugin matrix — an
uncovered plugin with **zero tests**. `AppBlockerStateStore` is already
bridge-free (it takes a `Context`, not a `PluginCall`), so **no refactor** is
needed — just the missing on-device coverage of the block-correctness logic a
mocked Chromium bridge can't exercise.

`AppBlockerStateStoreInstrumentedTest` drives it against the device's **real
SharedPreferences**:
- `save → load` round-trips the blocked package set (sorted) and the expiry;
- an indefinite block (no expiry) persists;
- an **elapsed** block is detected on load, **clears** the store, and reports
  inactive — the time-based behavior that decides whether an app is really still blocked;
- `isBlocked` reflects the saved set and goes false after `clear`.

42:Tee0,Closes #10553.

A redemption whose worker died mid-payout was stuck in `status='processing'` forever with **no auto-recovery**, contradicting the file's documented "automatic retry."

## Root cause
- The batch selector filters `status='approved'`, and the intended stale-lock recovery clause (`processing_started_at < now-LOCK_TIMEOUT`) was **unreachable dead code**: it's ANDed with `status='approved'`, but a locked row is `processing`, and `markFailed(retryable)` always resets `processing_started_at` to NULL when returning a row to `approved` — so no approved row ever carries a stale lock. (Same dead pattern was duplicated in `repositories/token-redemptions.ts`.)
- The per-redemption loop had **no try/catch**, and `executeEvmPayout` didn't wrap `writeContract`/`waitForTransactionReceipt` — one RPC throw aborted the whole batch with the row left locked.

## The double-pay trap
A naive recovery selector that re-approves any stuck `processing` row would **double-pay** a transfer that was broadcast but not yet confirmed. The fix is designed entirely around making that impossible to do automatically.

## Fix
1. **`broadcast_tx_hash` column** (migration **0155**, additive/idempotent), persisted the **moment** a transfer is submitted — *before* confirmation — so recovery can tell "never broadcast" (safe to retry) from "broadcast, unconfirmed" (reconcile, never re-broadcast). EVM records it right after `writeContract`; if persistence or confirmation then fails, the in-process path returns `needsReconciliation`, never `retryable`.
2. **`classifyStaleProcessingLock`** — pure, exported, exhaustively tested. The load-bearing safety decision: recorded hash → **reconcile**; Solana w/ no hash → **reconcile** (atomic send+confirm can't be proven un-sent); EVM w/ no hash → **re-approve** (worker died before submitting). `recoverStaleLocks` runs each batch and re-approves *only* provably-un-broadcast EVM locks via a guarded conditional UPDATE; everything else is parked `failed` + `requires_review` + an ops alert — **never auto-retried**.
3. **Batch-loop try/catch** (one bad row no longer aborts the batch; it's left locked for next-cycle recovery). `markFailed(retryable)` clears `broadcast_tx_hash` (a reverted tx moved nothing → safe fresh attempt). Dead clause removed from processor + repo; repo gains `getStaleProcessingLocks`.

## Evidence — 17 pass / 0 fail
- **Classifier** exhaustive over every network × hash state (no possibly-broadcast row is ever re-approved).
- **executeEvmPayout** proves the hash is persisted **between** broadcast and confirm (ordering asserted), and routes every failure safely: `writeContract` throw → retryable (never submitted); persist throw → reconcile; confirm throw → reconcile (live, unconfirmed); revert → retryable (moved nothing).
- **recoverStaleLocks** routing (EVM-null→approved; hash→failed+review+alert; Solana-null→reconcile) and **batch-loop isolation** (a mid-batch throw doesn't stop later rows).
- Existing payout suites (`payout-status-resilience`, `payout-fork`, `stripe-connect-payout`) still green. `bun run --cwd packages/cloud/shared typecheck` → exit 0. Biome clean.

## Residual (documented, not hidden)
- A hard process-kill in the sub-ms window between `writeContract` resolving and the hash commit is unrecoverable without pre-broadcast idempotency keys — but that window is vastly smaller than today's "any crash never recovers."
- Solana is conservatively routed to reconciliation rather than refactored into sign→sendRawTransaction→confirm (which would let it persist a pre-confirmation signature like EVM); flagged as a follow-up.

> ⚠️ Money-path change — **not self-merged.** Maintainer review/merge per the launch protocol. Tracked on the coordination board #10561.43:T6c8,## Problem (the e2e-coverage ratchet is RED on develop)
