This decrements the #8801 coverage debt by wiring one more surface plugin's keyless e2e, and unbreaks the gate.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)44:T1369,Closes the deferred **#3** of the final money/security scan #10279 (server-wallet provision squatting) and **reverts a regression** introduced by #10382's attempt at the same item. #10279 **#4** (anon free-message reservation) shipped in #10382 and is kept as-is.

## The regression in #10382 (develop/staging only, not prod)

#10382 dropped the global-unique on `client_address`, added composite `UNIQUE(organization_id, client_address, chain_type)`, and **org-scoped the RPC lookup to `authenticatedUser.organization_id`**. That breaks **every** legitimate server-wallet RPC call:

- **provision** stores the row under the **API-key owner's org** (`requireUserOrApiKey`).
- **RPC** authenticates by wallet signature → `findOrCreateUserByWalletAddress` resolves a **separate, wallet-derived `wallet-${address}` org**.
- The agent's `clientAddress` is a freshly-generated key, not the human's login wallet, so those orgs are always different → `executeServerWalletRpc`'s `where client_address = X AND organization_id = <wallet-org>` matches nothing → `ServerWalletNotFoundError` (404) on every real call.
- #10382's test can't catch it: it mocks `findFirst` to return a wallet regardless and only asserts the WHERE *contains* `organization_id` (an assertion that's vacuous anyway, since a column back-references its table).
- Its migration `0153_agent_server_wallets_org_scoped_client_address.sql` was **never added to `_journal.json`** (orphan) — so it never applied; the DB is still global-unique from migration `0038`.

## The correct fix — proof-of-control at provision (issue option 1)

`client_address` is a **global capability key by design** (the elizacloud client documents that RPC "verifies the signature against the `client_address` registered at provision time"; the agent holds the key). So keep the global unique + global RPC lookup and add the one missing thing — **proof the provisioner controls the key**:

- **Revert #10382:** delete the orphan migration, restore `client_address` global-unique in the schema (matches the un-migrated DB → **no new migration**), revert `executeServerWalletRpc` + `rpc/route.ts` to the global lookup by `client_address`, remove dead `getOrganizationIdForClientAddress`.
- **Wire contract:** `@elizaos/cloud-sdk` `buildWalletProvisionChallenge` — the exact string the client signs and the server rebuilds (single source of truth).
- **Server gate** (`server-wallets.ts`): `provisionServerWallet` verifies the signature over the challenge with the `clientAddress` key (real `viem.verifyMessage`) **before** any Steward/DB work — freshness window + nonce replay (degrades gracefully on a cache outage since re-provision is idempotent). Errors → 400/401/409.
- **Provision route:** `controlProof` is a required, validated body field; `clientAddress` is EVM-validated regardless of `chainType` (it's always the agent's EVM key).
- **Client** (`cloud-wallet.ts` / `bridge-client.ts`): signs the challenge with the local client key and sends `controlProof`; aborts if the local key ≠ requested address.

A squatter can no longer claim an address it doesn't control, so the global-unique row always belongs to the true key-holder — the DoS **and** the cross-tenant RPC mis-resolution are both gone, and the RPC lookup stays an unambiguous global lookup that works across the provision-org/RPC-org split.

## Evidence

`.github/issue-evidence/10279-money-security-followups.md`. `bun --conditions=eliza-source test` — 11/11:

- `server-wallets-provision-proof.test.ts` (6) — **real viem keypairs/signatures**; only Steward/DB/cache doubled. Accepts a valid proof; rejects forged-by-other-key (the squatting case, no Steward/DB), expired, far-future, replayed, and chainType-mismatch proofs.
- `wallet-provision-challenge.test.ts` (4) — wire-format golden (pins the exact bytes).
- `server-wallets.test.ts` (1) — **rewritten** to render the WHERE to SQL and assert the lookup is **global** by `client_address`, **not** org-scoped (the prior object-walk assertion was vacuous).
- Gated live e2e (`group-b-account-billing.test.ts`, `RUN_STEWARD_WALLET_E2E=1`): happy path sends a real signed proof; added "proof required → 400" and "proof not signed by clientAddress → 401".
- develop's #4 suites pass unchanged on this branch.

**Verify:** `tsgo` clean on cloud-sdk / cloud-shared / cloud-api / plugin-elizacloud (0 errors); biome clean. No migration added (DB already global-unique; orphan deleted, schema reverted to match).

**Screenshots:** N/A — cloud-backend money/security change, no UI surface.

## ⚠️ BREAKING (intentional, security control)

Provisioning now **requires** a control-proof. Agents on a `@elizaos/plugin-elizacloud` build predating this cannot provision **new** wallets until they upgrade (already-provisioned wallets and all RPC are unaffected). A security control that's optional is no control.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)48:Td62,Follow-up to **#10380** (DB-backed inference pending-charge ledger), which merged to `develop`. After that merge I ran a **5-lens / 3-vote adversarial review** over the new money-critical ledger; it found the first cut's headline guarantees were **not actually held on real Postgres**. This PR fixes them. The ledger is dormant behind the default-off `INFERENCE_BILLING_LEDGER` flag, so `develop` was never at risk — but these must land before any cutover.

## What the review found, and the fix

| Confirmed defect (votes) | Fix |
|---|---|
| **Overdraw bound was FALSE (HIGH, 3/3 ×2 lenses).** The admission's in-flight `SUM` reads a stale MVCC snapshot under READ COMMITTED — `FOR UPDATE` on the org row does **not** serialize a cross-table `SUM`, so a same-org burst over-admits. (Single-connection PGlite masked it.) | Admission now runs in `writeTransaction` behind a per-org `pg_advisory_xact_lock` (the idiomatic pattern here — see `affiliates.ts`, `redeemable-earnings.ts`), so each admission reads the `SUM` only after concurrent ones **commit**. |
| **Lost charge on crash (HIGH, 3/3).** claim + debit were separate auto-commit statements; a crash between them stranded the row `settled` with no debit, unrecoverable by the sweep. | claim + debit now run in **one transaction** — a crash rolls back the claim, the row stays `pending`, the sweep recovers it. Also closes the settle-window transient over-admit. |
| **Timezone skew (HIGH/LOW, 3/3).** sweep cutoff was a client UTC ISO string vs a `timestamp`-without-tz column. | cutoff computed **in SQL** (`enqueued_at < NOW() − interval`). |
| **Flag-flip orphan (MED, 3/3).** | cron sweeps **both** backends every run (idempotent). |
| **Unbounded growth / immortal `request_id` (LOW, 3/3).** | sweep GCs terminal rows past a 24h retention window. |

The debit is replicated from `reserveAndDeductCredits` (same atomic `FOR UPDATE` guard + `credit_transactions` row) so it can run inside the claim transaction — `deductCredits` can't take an external tx; cache invalidation fires post-commit. A confirmed **parity residual** (shared with the KV backstop — `billUsage` throwing after output → `settle(0)` under-bills one request) is a product-semantics call on abort billing and is documented, not silently changed.

## Evidence

- **97 pass / 0 fail** across touched + related suites (all real-DB PGlite), incl. **+3 new** tests: in-flight self-correction, dropped-inline → sweep recovery (the lost-charge path), and GC retention; the cron test now asserts both-backend sweep.
- **Overdraw bound re-measured** (`.github/issue-evidence/9899-db-ledger-overdraw-measurement.json`): bursts of 50×\$3 / 20×\$10 / 100×\$1 prevent **\$50 / \$100 / \$75** of overdraw; in-flight ≤ balance; final balance ≥ 0 in every case.
- Typecheck + Biome clean on changed files. Review findings + fixes catalogued in `.github/issue-evidence/9899-db-ledger.md`; corrected design + a Tier-3 hardening note in `docs/inference-hot-path.md`.

> Note: a separate `develop` issue surfaced during rebase — a concurrent PR's `0153_agent_server_wallets_org_scoped_client_address.sql` is on `develop` but **missing from the Drizzle journal** (orphaned, will never run). It is unrelated to this PR (my migration won the `0153` slot via #10380) and is being tracked for a focused renumber-to-0154 fix.

## The chain (all real)
```
desktop mic → DesktopMicSource → FusedWakeManager (electrobun main, bun:ffi)
  → real OpenWakeWordGgmlModel.scoreFrame (libwakeword) → threshold+streak+refractory
  → onWake(confidence) → sendToWebview('voice:fusedWake', {stage:'head-fired', confidence})
  → registerDesktopFusedWake → emitFusedWake() → window 'eliza:fused-wake'
  → useWakeController → useWakeListenWindow.onOpen
  → useShellController: setIsOpen(true) + startCapture('converse')   → BAR ACTIVE
```

## What's built
**Producer (plugin-local-inference):** `OpenWakeWordDetector.onWake` now carries the real classifier `confidence`; new `WakeFireEvent{stage:'head-fired',confidence}`; engine forwards it.
**Desktop arming (electrobun, T1):** `FusedWakeManager` (mirrors `SwabbleManager`) runs the real standalone libwakeword head in the desktop Bun main process and emits `voice:fusedWake` via `sendToWebview`; inert (`started:false`) when the model isn't staged, so it never forces mic access without the on-device assets. Wired through `native/index.ts`, `rpc-handlers`, `rpc-schema`, and a narrow `@elizaos/plugin-local-inference/voice-wake` barrel (no heavy-engine import edge into the lean main bundle).
**Transport + renderer:** `fused-wake-desktop-bridge.ts` forwards the channel → `emitFusedWake`, sets `__ELIZA_FUSED_WAKE__`, and arms `fusedWake:start` at boot (`main.tsx`). Off-desktop it's a no-op (Swabble fallback untouched).

Scope: `head-fired` only — the standalone openWakeWord head is a single trained-head detector; the two-stage producers don't exist and are out of scope. `head-fired` fully drives the bar.

Fixes the remaining plugin-music MUSIC umbrella dispatch path from #10470 by removing the English text/regex playback fallback from the umbrella action.

When the planner does not provide an explicit `action`, `op`, or `subaction`, `MUSIC` now asks `runtime.useModel(ModelType.TEXT_LARGE)` for a structured `<response><action>...</action></response>` enum and parses it with `parseKeyValueXml`. Explicit structured params and structural machine-format checks still take precedence. `validate()` stays structural only and does not classify natural-language text or call the model.

## Why

The old fallback reused `inferOpFromText` / transport regexes in the umbrella dispatcher, so natural-language English snippets could bypass LLM-driven action selection. This made the path brittle and language-specific.

4e:Tdf1,## What & why (on-device finding)

Pursuing #9943's named gap — "no sleep-wake / app-backgrounding lifecycle spec ... uncovered on every platform" — I drove the **real running app** on an API-34 emulator (`ai.elizaos.app` WebView, attached over CDP-via-adb) through a real background→foreground cycle and found a **silent breakage**, not just missing coverage:

```
direct Capacitor appStateChange (isActive) events: []          <- appStateChange SILENT
raw document.visibilitychange states: ["hidden","visible"]     <- visibilitychange RELIABLE
app eliza:app-pause/resume events recorded: []                 <- app lifecycle DEAD on background
```

`mobile-lifecycle.ts` derives `APP_PAUSE_EVENT`/`APP_RESUME_EVENT` **solely** from the Capacitor `@capacitor/app` `appStateChange` listener. Those events gate real work — e.g. `retained-lazy.tsx` prunes backgrounded views to reclaim memory on `APP_PAUSE_EVENT`. On Android, `appStateChange` is unreliable/silent (and under plugin-load contention the App plugin even reports `"App" plugin is not implemented on android`), so pause/resume — and the memory-reclaiming prune — **never run on background**. The code already *catches* the App plugin being unavailable (`logNativePluginUnavailable("App", …)`) but never recovers.

## Fix

Add a `document.visibilitychange` fallback driving the same pause/resume events, **deduped** with `appStateChange` via one `setAppActive` transition gate (never double-fires when both signals are present), registered idempotently at module scope (no listener leak on re-init/HMR). `visibilitychange` is the W3C-standard signal that fires on every surface (web/desktop/iOS/Android WebView) — proven on-device here.

## Verification

- **Unit** — `mobile-lifecycle.test.ts` **12/12** (3 new: `visibilitychange→hidden` ⇒ `APP_PAUSE_EVENT`, `→visible` ⇒ `APP_RESUME_EVENT`, and `appStateChange`+`visibilitychange` same-transition ⇒ **one** dispatch).
- **On-device** — `.github/issue-evidence/9943-android-lifecycle-fallback/` (CDP probe transcript + reusable script + running-app screenshot): `visibilitychange` fires on real backgrounding while `appStateChange` does not.
- **Honest caveat** — the end-to-end *fixed* behavior couldn't be captured on-device because `build:android` is currently broken on this host (`@tailwindcss/vite` missing + an eliza/Milady workspace path crossover), so a fresh APK with this change can't be produced here. The probe runs against the prebuilt APK (old lifecycle), which is why it shows the broken state; the unit test proves the new handler dispatches the events.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)

---

## Update: same bug in the network listener (second on-device finding)

Toggling connectivity on-device (`svc wifi/data off`/`on`) found the Capacitor `@capacitor/network` plugin is **absent from the Android WebView bridge** (`hasNetworkPlugin:false`, `getStatus:"no-plugin"`), so `initializeNetworkListener` — which derives `NETWORK_STATUS_CHANGE_EVENT` (the WebSocket reconnect scheduler's airplane-mode signal) **solely** from `Network.addListener` — never fires on a connectivity change. `window` `online`/`offline` fire reliably (`["offline","online"]` on toggle).

Added the symmetric `window online/offline` fallback (deduped via a `setConnected` transition gate, idempotent module-scope registration). Test suite now **15/15**. On-device evidence: `cdp-network-probe.txt`/`.mjs`.

(This PR also now includes the entrypoint wiring that mounts the lifecycle in `main.tsx`.)4f:Tf7b,## What

Closes the synthetic-event gap from #9953 Phase 2. The renderer already consumed
`eliza:fused-wake` (`useWakeController` + `fused-wake-bridge`), but **nothing
produced it** — the native `OpenWakeWordDetector` fired only into
`prewarmConversation`, so the bottom bar was driven only by the Swabble
Web-Speech fallback. This adds the producer seam end-to-end.

## The bridge

```
real libwakeword.dylib (bun:ffi)               packages/native/plugins/wakeword-cpp
  → OpenWakeWordGgmlModel.scoreFrame()          plugins/…/voice/wake-word-ggml.ts (production binding)
  → OpenWakeWordDetector (sustain + refractory)  plugins/…/voice/wake-word.ts      (onWake now carries confidence)
  → bridgeDetectorToFusedWake(sink)             plugins/…/voice/fused-wake-bridge.ts  ← NEW producer bridge
  → engine.startVoiceSession({ wakeWord.onFusedWake })  plugins/…/services/engine.ts  ← host seam
  → window "eliza:fused-wake" (FusedWakeEventDetail)    @elizaos/shared/events        ← one shared contract
  → useWakeController → useWakeListenWindow     packages/ui/…/voice               (bar opens + a turn starts)
```

- **One contract, no drift.** `FUSED_WAKE_EVENT` + `FusedWakeEventDetail` now
  live in `@elizaos/shared/events` and are imported by **both** the producer
  (`@elizaos/plugin-local-inference`) and the consumer (`@elizaos/ui`). The
  renderer `fused-wake-bridge` re-uses the shared type instead of a local copy.
- `OpenWakeWordDetector.onWake` now carries the firing `confidence` (additive;
  existing `() => …` callbacks still type-check).

## Real `libwakeword` ran locally — not a documented-only handoff

`libwakeword` is a pure-C `bun:ffi` library that **builds + runs on this host**
(macOS, Apple Silicon). Evidence in `.github/issue-evidence/10351-fused-wake-bridge/`:

- **Real native E2E (headline):** `validate-fused-wake-e2e.mjs` + `fused-wake-e2e.log`
  — **2/2.** Real `libwakeword.dylib` + sha-pinned `hey-eliza` GGUFs + a real PCM
  clip → `OpenWakeWordGgmlModel` (bun:ffi) → `OpenWakeWordDetector` →
  `bridgeDetectorToFusedWake` → `eliza:fused-wake` window event → shipped
  `wakeControllerReducer` → `head-fast-path` `WakeDetection` (= the bar opens +
  a turn starts). `"hey eliza"` peak `P(wake)=1.0000` fires; `"what time is it"`
  peak `0.0056` does not.
- **Producer bridge (CI):** `plugins/…/voice/fused-wake-bridge.test.ts` — 4/4,
  drives the real `OpenWakeWordDetector` (scripted model) → asserts exactly one
  `head-fired` event per firing with the firing confidence; refractory / re-arm /
  never-fire covered. Node, no native.
- **Renderer activation (CI):** `useWakeController.fused.test.tsx` — a head-fired
  `eliza:fused-wake` opens the listening window (`useWakeListenWindow.onOpen`).
- **Audio:** `hey-eliza-clip.wav` — the exact real clip fed to the runtime.

The `eliza:fused-wake` window CustomEvent collapsed into one process by the E2E
harness is the **identical** seam used in production; only the cross-process
transport is the device step.

no longer cherry-picks cleanly, so each change was verified against develop's
current integration points).

   - Camera now only appears on the AOSP ElizaOS build (`isAospShellEnabled()`),
     matching the home tile — filtered from the view manager
     (`useAvailableViews`) and gated on the `/camera` route (`App.tsx`), so it
     is hidden on web, desktop, iOS, and stock Play-Store Android.
   - Drops the redundant, broken `voice` `BuiltinTab` alias (it pointed the
     route at the wrong Settings section). **Voice itself is unchanged** — it
     still lives in the chat controls and Settings → Voice. Only the dead route,
     launcher icon, parity-catalog route entry (the Settings → Voice *section*
     is kept), audit slug, and background-fuzz fixtures are removed.

2. **Move smartglasses + facewear config into Settings → Wearables** (`05e980`)
   - `plugin-facewear/src/register.ts` registers one `wearables` settings
     section (`WearablesSettingsSection`, via `registerSettingsSection`) that
     lazy-loads `FacewearView` + `SmartglassesView` as two tabs.
   - The served view defs in `index.ts` keep only the `xr`/`tui` modalities with
     `visibleInManager:false` + `desktopTabEnabled:false`, so the agent still
     serves the in-headset XR view host and terminal surfaces and **all
     `FACEWEAR_*`/`SMARTGLASSES_*`/`XR_*` actions still resolve**.
   - `FacewearView` "Manage" now switches to the smartglasses sibling tab via a
     `wearables:select-tab` event instead of navigating to the removed route.
   - Deletes the orphan `XRSettingsSection.tsx` (zero remaining references on
     develop) and updates the route/interaction/visual coverage gates + the
     device-interactions e2e to the new location.

3. **Embed the full Feed web app authenticated as the agent on GUI** (`f5cb066`)
   - Adds a reusable `EmbeddedAppViewer` to `@elizaos/ui` (iframe + `*_READY` →
     auth `postMessage` handshake, sharing the `viewer-auth` helpers): given a
     run's `viewer.url` + `viewer.authMessage` (the FEED_AUTH session token), it
     loads the app and posts the token back **only to the verified http(s)
     origin (fails closed otherwise)**.
   - `FeedView` renders the embed **only on the GUI modality**; XR and TUI keep
     the `FeedSpatialView` operator dashboard (a cross-origin iframe can't render
     there), and the 12s dashboard poll is skipped on the embed path.
   - **Import-guard fixes (squashed in):** `EmbeddedAppViewer` is imported from
     the bare `@elizaos/ui` barrel (the only specifier the view-bundle import
     guard's `HOST_EXTERNAL_IMPORTERS` allowlist permits — a deep subpath would
     externalize an import the host can never rewrite) and exported from **both**
     `@elizaos/ui` entries (`index.ts` AND `browser.ts`), so it resolves in the
     view-bundle runtime loader and the app renderer build alike.

- `XRSettingsSection` has **zero** remaining references after deletion (grep
  clean across `packages/` + `plugins/`).

Re-validation of the benchmark suite on **`gpt-oss-120b` / Cerebras** for #10193 (the baseline half), plus the one harness bug it surfaced.

### Bug fixed — MINT code grader silently fails every code task
`benchmarks/mint/upstream/mint/utils/exec.py` ran the untrusted solution in `multiprocessing.Process(target=unsafe_execute)` where `unsafe_execute` is a **local closure** (the OpenAI human-eval pattern, which assumes `fork`). Under macOS / Python 3.14's default **`spawn`** start method the target is pickled → `Can't pickle local object` → the process never runs → every code subtask (`humaneval`, `mbpp`) scored as failed **regardless of model output**. Fixed with an explicit `fork` context (fallback to default where fork is unavailable).

Without the fix MINT's code subtasks are unconditionally 0; with it, MINT through the eliza harness on Cerebras grades a real **0.417 (5/12)**.

## Evidence (real model, no mocks)
`.github/issue-evidence/10193-cerebras-gpt-oss-120b-revalidation.md` — full run with live-call/trajectory proof:

| Benchmark | Score | Path |
|---|---|---|
| mmlu | 0.925 (37/40) | direct |
| gsm8k | 0.975 (39/40) | direct |
| humaneval | 1.000 (30/30, sandbox-exec, verified real code) | direct |
| mt_bench | 0.85 (Cerebras judge) | direct |
| bfcl | **0.567** (12/20) — refutes the matrix's flat `1.00` | direct |
| mint | **0.417** (5/12) — only after this fix | eliza harness |

The headline #10193 finding: the committed `RESULTS_MATRIX.md` flat `1.00` rows do **not** reproduce on a real Cerebras `gpt-oss-120b` run (`bfcl` is 0.567); the one real `1.00` (`humaneval`) spot-checks to a correct, sandbox-executed, model-authored solution. Trajectories carry real `reasoning_tokens` + latency under `model=gpt-oss-120b`.

- `19 passed` — `pytest benchmarks/mint/tests/ -k "exec or executor or correct or code"`.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)58:Te60,## What changed

`MANAGE_PLUGINS` (the plugin-manager umbrella action in `packages/core/src/features/plugin-manager/actions/plugin.ts`) used to pick its subaction by **regex-matching the user's natural-language `message.content.text`**. `inferMode` ran a battery of English keyword tables — `INSTALL_VERBS`, `EJECT_VERBS`, `LIST_VERBS`, `DETAILS_VERBS`, `ENABLE_VERBS`, `DISABLE_VERBS`, `CREATE_VERBS`, `PLUGIN_NOUN`, `MANAGE_VERBS`, … — to DECIDE which of the ~13 subactions (install / eject / sync / reinject / list / list_ejected / search / details / status / enable / disable / core_status / create) to run.

This migrates the whole `inferMode` block to the shared **`resolveActionArgs` + `SubactionsMap`** substrate (`packages/core/src/actions/resolve-action-args.ts`) — the canonical pattern already used by `plugin-goals`' `OWNER_GOALS`:

- Declares `PLUGIN_SUBACTIONS: SubactionsMap<PluginSubaction>` with each subaction's required/optional params.
- **Structured planner/programmatic `action` enum** (including the legacy `mode` alias and the `normalizeSubaction` aliases) routes directly with **no model call** — the planner-trust fast path.
- **Natural-language** requests now run a **single `TEXT_LARGE` extraction pass** that picks the subaction from the structured enum and pulls its params — instead of English keyword regexes.
- The **machine extractors are kept**: `extractNameFromText` / `extractQueryFromText` / `normalizePluginNameInput` / `readSourceOption` parse structured plugin identifiers (`@scope/plugin-x`, `plugin-x`), git URLs, and search queries — token-shape extraction, not behavior-deciding NL matching. Their results are **seeded into the resolver params** so required fields resolve even when the LLM does not surface them verbatim.
- No-match degrades to the same `"Tell me which plugin operation to run…"` clarification (no regression to a bare delete).
- The **create choice-reply multi-turn path** and the **owner/role validate gate** are untouched.

## Why (the i18n smell, #10471)

Per #10471: deciding agent behavior by matching the user's free-form `message.content.text` against English keywords is a smell. Matching the planner/model's structured English-enum **output** is a keep. `inferMode`'s `*_VERBS` / `*_NOUN` regex tables matched the **user's text** to decide behavior — exactly the smell. The structured-enum path (`action` / `mode` enum, with `normalizeSubaction` aliases) matched the **planner output** and is preserved as the fast path.

## Verification

- `bun x tsc --noEmit -p packages/core/tsconfig.json` — 0 errors for touched files.
- `bun x @biomejs/biome lint` on both files — clean.
- Tests (`packages/core`), green **8/8**:
  - `plugin-manager-umbrella.test.ts` (existing surface tests) — unchanged, still pass.
  - `plugin-manager-routing.test.ts` (**new**, drives the real handler with a stubbed `plugin_manager` service + deterministic mock `useModel`):
    - structured planner `action` enum → dispatch with **no** model call;
    - legacy `mode: "installed"` alias normalizes to `list`, no model call;
    - NL `"install @elizaos/plugin-discord"` → LLM picks `install`, the machine-parsed name `@elizaos/plugin-discord` reaches the install handler;
    - no-match request → structured `clarify` reply, no dispatch.

## Behavior change

Free-form plugin-management utterances are now routed by one structured LLM extraction pass rather than English keyword regexes. Structured/planner calls (the `action`/`mode` enum) behave identically and still skip the model. The clarification fallback message is unchanged.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)59:T7ef,## What

Adds the one piece of #9581's "Windows robustness/input tail" the issue still lists as remaining — **"non-disruptive mouse/keyboard effect evidence"** — as a real **screen recording**. The existing `capture-windows-desktop-evidence.mjs` harness proves Windows CUA input lands by reading the typed marker back through the clipboard; this is the moving-picture companion.

## How

`plugins/plugin-computeruse/scripts/record-windows-cua-input.mjs` (+ `record:windows-cua-input` script):
- launches `notepad.exe` → resolves the real window → **maximizes** it (so a click at display-center always lands on it and foregrounds it),
- drives `mouse_move` → `click` → **progressive chunked `type`** of a marker phrase (a frame is captured after each chunk so the recording shows text appearing),
- `ctrl+a` to select, then verifies via `ctrl+c` + clipboard read-back,
- captures the frame burst through the computeruse WinRT/.NET capture path (`gdigrab` is blocked in this RDP session) and assembles it into an mp4 + gif with ffmpeg.

Non-disruptive: it drives a freshly-launched Notepad and terminates it by window id; it never touches the user's apps.

Captured on a real Windows 11 Pro host (QEMU), 1728×1052, `verified: true` (marker read back from Notepad):

- `initial-empty-notepad.png`, `recording-summary.json`.

**Fidelity note:** the legacy PowerShell/user32 input driver (used when nutjs is unavailable) lower-cases letters and drops some symbols, so the visible phrase reads `elizaOS cua on windows -- 9581 …`; the lowercase-+-digits marker (`eliza-win-cua-<ts>`) round-trips exactly and is what the read-back verifies — input demonstrably reaches the window.

Live `gpt-oss-120b` graded rerun and HITL multi-Codex/account trajectory review remain outside this deterministic local slice; this PR adds the static inventory and closes local discovery/calibration gaps needed before that live run.5b:Tb35,Closes #10196. Salvaged from `feat/10196-view-heap-soak` (no PR), with the three review nits fixed.

## Problem

The bounded module/view caches already size off `isCacheMemoryConstrained()`, but the caps only take effect when a prune actually runs — and the **only** memory-driven prune trigger the shell had was the non-standard `memorypressure` window event, which **Chromium never fires**. So live heap had no path into eviction at all.

## What lands

- `heap-pressure-monitor.ts` — a single shared poll (`installHeapPressureMonitor`, one `setInterval` at 10s, paused while the tab is hidden) that reads `performance.memory` and, when `usedJSHeapSize` crosses `HEAP_PRESSURE_RATIO` (0.8 of `jsHeapSizeLimit`), dispatches `eliza:heap-pressure`. `DynamicViewLoader` + `retained-lazy` force-evict idle entries on it. No-op on Safari/Firefox (no `performance.memory`) — those keep relying on TTL/visibility/app-pause.
- `readJsHeap()` in `bounded-view-lru.ts` — the single boundary read (non-standard `performance.memory` validated, not `any`), routed through the existing `isCacheMemoryConstrained()` seam via `isHeapUnderPressure()`.
- Centralizes the remote-bundle caps (6/2) that had silently diverged from retained-lazy.
- `jsHeapUsedSize` added to cache telemetry.

## Review fixes (second commit)

- **Actually consolidated the heap reader** — `ViewTelemetryProfiler` now uses the shared `readJsHeap()` instead of its own private `readJsHeapUsedSize()`. (The bounded-view-lru doc claimed "exactly one heap read implementation"; now it's true.)
- Corrected the `DynamicViewLoader` comment that wrongly credited `ViewTelemetryProfiler` with dispatching the event — the shared monitor dispatches it.
- `retained-lazy.test.tsx` `afterEach` now calls `__resetHeapPressureMonitorForTests()` so the poll interval + `visibilitychange` listener don't leak across the suite.

## Safety

Graceful degradation: `readJsHeap()` returns `null` on SSR/Safari/Firefox → larger caps retained, never falsely starved. A hard `refCount > 0` floor protects the active view (only idle `refCount === 0` entries evict — no thrash). Shared `setInterval`, no busy-poll.

## Evidence

```
$ bunx vitest run src/retained-lazy.test.tsx src/state/bounded-view-lru.heap.test.ts
 Test Files  2 passed (2)
      Tests  9 passed (9)
```
Tests drive the real LRU + event path (not mocked units): the heap-pressure test stubs `performance.memory` to 950/1000, dispatches `eliza:heap-pressure`, asserts the idle module is evicted with `reason: "heap-pressure"` and the live `jsHeapUsedSize: 950` rides the telemetry event.

Typecheck: clean for all touched files.

`audit:app` visual review: **N/A** — this is view-cache eviction + telemetry plumbing; zero rendered-output change (no component renders differently).

On June 30, 2026, the ElizaOS team focused on hardening agent stability and security, successfully transitioning core operations to structured action routing to improve reliability. Significant progress was also made in cloud infrastructure monetization and expanding native Android plugin testing coverage.

## ✅ Completed Work

### Agent Stability & Security
*   **Crash Recovery:** Implemented a memory watchdog and crash-recovery harness to prevent OOM crashes and ensure clean restarts ([elizaos/eliza#10375](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/10375)).
*   **PII Protection:** Deployed an end-to-end PII/secret swap layer, including detection, nonce management, and egress verification ([elizaos/eliza#10551](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/10551)).
*   **Orchestration:** Integrated watchdog round-trip detection and spend-cap warnings into the orchestrator ([elizaos/eliza#10323](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/10323)).

### Structured Action Routing
*   **Reliability Improvements:** Migrated core and plugin actions (Music, Browser, Linear) from regex-based fallbacks to structured planner enums and parameters ([elizaos/eliza#10546](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/10546), [elizaos/eliza#10595](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/10595), [elizaos/eliza#10592](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/10592)).
*   **System Cleanup:** Removed inert keyword specifications and dead action-rescue subsystems to streamline the codebase ([elizaos/eliza#10547](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/10547), [elizaos/eliza#10586](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/10586)).

### Cloud & App Infrastructure
*   **Monetization Hardening:** Improved Stripe Connect payout logic to mitigate double-debits and IDOR vulnerabilities ([elizaos/eliza#10327](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/10327), [elizaos/eliza#10571](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/10571)).
*   **Performance Optimization:** Enhanced the inference hot path via pricing read caching and IAC fast-paths for `/v1/messages` ([elizaos/eliza#10583](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/10583), [elizaos/eliza#10587](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/10587)).
*   **Onboarding:** Streamlined user onboarding by integrating it directly into the `ContinuousChatOverlay` ([elizaos/eliza#10302](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/10302)).

### Native Plugin Testing
*   **Android Coverage:** Expanded on-device `androidTest` coverage for native plugins (Location, Wi-Fi, AppBlocker, System) to ensure hardware-level reliability ([elizaos/eliza#10406](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/10406), [elizaos/eliza#10437](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/10437), [elizaos/eliza#10445](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/10445)).

67:T8ce,## What

Upgrades `@biomejs/biome` from **2.4.16 → 2.5.1** (latest stable) across the entire workspace and reformats the tree to 2.5.1's canonical style.

## Why

The workspace had a **biome version skew**: `package.json` files declared a mix of `2.4.16`, `2.5.0`, and `^2.4.14`, while `bun.lock` (and therefore CI's `bun install --frozen-lockfile`) resolved **2.4.16**. Because PR CI lint is path-gated (it only lints changed files), 2.5.x-formatted files merged via PRs that didn't touch them could slip in and then fail a full repo `bun run lint`. Pinning one version everywhere removes that whole class of intermittent lint drift.

## Changes

**Commit 1 — version bump (config only):**
- Pin `@biomejs/biome` to `2.5.1` in all **97** `package.json` files
- Regenerate `bun.lock` (resolves `2.4.16` → `2.5.1`)
- Bump `$schema` to `2.5.1` in all **68** `biome.json` configs
- Migrate the deprecated `linter.rules.recommended: true` → `linter.rules.preset: "recommended"` in **50** configs (behavior-identical; removes the 2.5.x deprecation diagnostic)

**Commit 2 — mechanical reformat:**
- `biome check --write` under the 2.5.1 toolchain (formatting + organizeImports only — **no logic changes**). The bulk of the diff is the trailing-comma behavior change between 2.4 and 2.5.

## Verification

- **Per-package lint green at 2.5.1:** serial sweep of all no-write first-party packages → **51 pass, 0 needing fixes, 0 residual lint errors**.
- `packages/core` typechecks clean (the reformat is format + import-ordering, which cannot change types).
- All `biome.json` configs validate at 2.5.1 (no `deserialize`/deprecation diagnostics; verified core/agent/app).

## Notes for the reviewer

- This is a large (~1100-file) **mechanical** change. The meaningful review surface is **commit 1** (versions + config). Commit 2 is pure formatter output.
- It's a repo-wide reformat, so it can go stale against an active `develop`. If it can't fast-forward at merge time, the cleanest sync is to **regenerate** rather than rebase: `bun install` then `bunx @biomejs/biome check --write .` on the latest `develop`. (At open time it rebased onto `develop` with **zero** conflicts.)

6d:Tc5c,## What & why

Closes the **deepest** part of #9967: "the Kotlin that runs on a phone is executed by
no test, on no device." Before this PR the entire `plugin-native-*` set had **zero**
Android instrumented tests; the only coverage drove the React views against a **mocked
`Capacitor.Plugins` bridge in desktop Chromium**, so the real Kotlin — the launcher's
Phone/SMS/Home/Assistant gating — ran untested.

Root cause of *why* it was untested: the native logic was welded to the Capacitor
`Plugin`/`Bridge`/`PluginCall` objects, which only exist inside a running app, so it
could not be asserted from `androidTest`. This PR fixes that at the source.

### Changes
- **`RoleStatus`** (added in the prior commit): the launcher/comms role read is
  extracted out of `SystemPlugin` into a pure, `Context`-taking object — `RoleManager`
  / `TelecomManager` / `PackageManager` / `Telephony` reads with **no** bridge
  dependency. `SystemPlugin.getStatus` just delegates, so runtime behavior is unchanged.
- **`RoleStatusInstrumentedTest`** now lives in the plugin's *own* `androidTest` source
  set (`@elizaos/capacitor-system`) and runs the real `RoleStatus.read()` against the
  device's actual system services — the first on-device test in the native set.
- **plugin `build.gradle`** gains `AndroidJUnitRunner` + `androidTest` deps so the
  plugin library is instrumentable **standalone** — no full-app build (voice/inference
  JNI, fused `.so`, omnivoice submodule) is needed to run it. That's the pattern the
  rest of the native-plugin × view matrix can follow.

## Evidence — real Pixel 6a (Android 16)

```
> Task :system:connectedDebugAndroidTest
Starting 3 tests on Pixel 6a - 16
Finished 3 tests on Pixel 6a - 16
BUILD SUCCESSFUL
testsuite ... tests="3" failures="0" errors="0" skipped="0"
```

The three tests prove the read returns **live** device state, not a stub:
- `read_reportsRealPackageNameFromContext` — packageName comes from the real `Context`.
- `read_enumeratesAllFourRolesFromRealRoleManager` — all 4 roles (home/dialer/sms/
  assistant) enumerate from the real `RoleManager`, each mapping to its constant.
- `read_homeHolderMatchesTheDeviceDefaultHome` — the home holder equals the device's
  actual default launcher (`com.google.android.apps.nexuslauncher`), cross-checked
  against `adb shell cmd role get-role-holders`.

Artifacts in `.github/issue-evidence/9967-androidtest-*`: gradle HTML report, JUnit XML,
`TestRunner` logcat, the live role-holder dump, device screenshot, and a screenrecord.

## Scope (honest)

This is the **foundational slice** of the #9967 work-order: it makes the previously
untestable native Kotlin testable and proves it on hardware, establishing the
standalone-instrumentation pattern. It does **not** yet cover the issue's other asks —
deterministic gradle wiring so all 9 native plugins compile into the launcher APK
(gap #1), the full native-plugin × view matrix across the remaining plugins, and the
launcher-as-home behavior tests. Those build directly on this pattern and are tracked
as the remaining #9967 surface.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)
6e:Teb4,## What

Lands the first **"Needs CI infra"** deliverable spun out of #9476 (tracked by #10333): run the MiniWoB++ benchmark suite against a **real Chromium engine** instead of JSDOM web mode.

The benchmark adapter was already engine-agnostic (`BrowserCommandExecutor`), so this is a **new executor + a gated lane — not a rewrite**. `report.engine` flips from `"jsdom-web"` to `"chromium"` and every action runs through a real browser process, mirroring how plugin-computeruse's OSWorld `*.real.test.ts` lanes drive a real OS.

## How

- **`src/benchmark/chromium-executor.ts`** — `createChromiumBenchmarkExecutor` (puppeteer-core, already a dep), `launchChromiumBenchmarkBrowser` (one browser per suite, fresh page per episode), `resolveChromiumExecutablePath` (env → playwright-core → system Chrome; `null` → lane self-skips). Command semantics mirror `browser-workspace-web.ts` **exactly** (type appends, fill replaces, check sets `checked`, get value/checked/text/count/url/title, snapshot, network-route via request interception with a hard external-network seal), so the **identical oracle sequences solve every task on both engines** — the engine-parity proof.
- **`src/benchmark/__tests__/miniwob-chromium.real.test.ts`** — gated `.real.test.ts` lane: oracle solves every task on every seed (reward 1), noop scores 0, on real Chromium.
- **`vitest.real.config.ts`** — opts the `.real.test.ts` lane back in (the root config excludes `**/*.real.test.ts`) while keeping the `@elizaos/*` source aliases. The "(or a dedicated config)" the issue called for.
- **`.github/workflows/browser-real-bench.yml`** — CI gate: `bunx playwright install --with-deps chromium`, run the lane + upload artifacts (nightly + dispatch + on benchmark changes), alongside the other heavy real lanes.
- **`scripts/run-miniwob-chromium-benchmark.mjs`** + `bench:miniwob:chromium` / `test:real-chromium` scripts.
- `BrowserWorkspaceMode` gains `"chromium"` (emitted only by this executor, never by `executeBrowserWorkspaceCommand`) so results carry an accurate engine tag.

## Evidence (real Chromium — `Google Chrome for Testing` via playwright)

```
$ bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-browser test:real-chromium
 ✓ src/benchmark/__tests__/miniwob-chromium.real.test.ts (2 tests) 84.5s
   ✓ oracle policy solves every task on every seed (reward 1) through real Chromium
   ✓ noop baseline scores 0 on real Chromium — reward discriminates, never auto-passes
 Test Files  1 passed (1)   Tests  2 passed (2)
```

Committed run artifacts in `.github/issue-evidence/10333-browser-real-chromium/`:

| Artifact | engine | policy | solved |
|----------|--------|--------|--------|
| `miniwob-chromium-oracle-run.json` | chromium | oracle | **18 / 18** (100%) |
| `miniwob-chromium-noop-run.json`   | chromium | noop   | **0 / 12** |

Reproduce:
```bash
bunx playwright install --with-deps chromium
bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-browser test:real-chromium
bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-browser bench:miniwob:chromium --policy oracle --seeds 3
```

Without a Chromium binary the lane self-skips and the script is a clean no-op, so the un-gated path stays green.

## Scope / still deferred on #10333

This PR delivers the **real-Chromium engine lane + its CI gate**. The other two #10333 items — an external-dataset benchmark (Mind2Web/WebArena) and a web-element grounding benchmark (ScreenSpot-Web/VisualWebBench) — need multi-GB external datasets / dockerized site environments and remain genuinely CI-infra-gated. Both extend through the same `BrowserCommandExecutor` seam this PR exercises.

Closes part of #10333 (real-Chromium lane + CI gate); leaves the issue open for the two dataset lanes.

6f:Tede,## What

Closes the one Definition-of-Done deliverable [verified missing on `develop`](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/issues/9476#issuecomment-4830133507) for #9476:

> **≥1 benchmark wired through `plugin-browser`** (mirror `plugin-computeruse/src/osworld/adapter.ts`), with a committed run artifact.

The other three DoD items already landed (CI-asserted parity matrix, JSDOM real-code lane, typed error contract). The web benchmarks (Mind2Web, WebShop, VisualWebBench) all **bypass** plugin-browser via the inference layer — no benchmark drove the BROWSER command surface end-to-end. This adds one that does.

## How

A **MiniWoB++-style** web-interaction benchmark (the canonical web-agent benchmark) under `plugins/plugin-browser/src/benchmark/`, the plugin-browser analog of `plugin-computeruse/src/osworld/`:

- **`adapter.ts`** — `BrowserBenchmarkAdapter` with the same observation/action/step seam as `OSWorldAdapter`, driving an engine-agnostic `BrowserCommandExecutor`. The workspace executor binds the **real** `executeBrowserWorkspaceCommand` router (the same mock-free path the `browser-workspace-web-real-code` lane uses) — no mock service stands in for the browser.
- **`tasks.ts`** — 6 tasks (`click-button`, `click-link`, `enter-text`, `enter-text-dynamic`, `click-checkboxes`, `multistep-purchase`). Pure markup, **no page scripts** (web mode hard-blocks script exec, GHSA-mhhr-9ph9-64j7); deterministic per seed; reward computed from **observable DOM state** read back through real BROWSER `get` commands (`value`/`checked`/`url`/`title`).
- **`policy.ts`** — `OraclePolicy` (deterministic solver) + `NoopPolicy`/`WrongPolicy` negative baselines.
- **`runner.ts`** — `runBenchmarkSuite` drives every task×seed episode, scores it, aggregates.
- **`__tests__/miniwob-adapter.test.ts`** — CI-asserted real-code lane (runs in the default `vitest run`).
- **`scripts/run-miniwob-benchmark.mjs`** + `bench:miniwob` — runnable harness that writes a JSON run report.

## Evidence (real, on a Windows 11 host) — see `.github/issue-evidence/9476-browser-benchmark/`

| Policy | Result | Proves |
|--------|--------|--------|
| oracle | **18/18 solved (100%)** | the suite is solvable end-to-end through real BROWSER commands; every action step records `resultMode: "web"` (the live router ran it) |
| noop   | **0/18** | doing nothing scores 0 — reward is grounded in real DOM state, not hard-coded |
| wrong  | **0/18** | wrong-target / wrong-text near-misses score 0 — reward discriminates |

Test lane locally: **5/5 passing**. Benchmark sources type-check clean (verified with a resolvable `@types/node`; the package's default `typecheck` hits a pre-existing worktree `typeRoots` quirk unrelated to this change). The broader plugin-browser suite's 8 core-barrel-dependent suites can't load under this box's light-install (a pre-existing `uuid`→`drizzle-orm`→`yaml`→`@noble/*` cascade, identical before this change); all 80 runnable tests pass, including the new lane.

## Reproduce

```bash
bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-browser test -- src/benchmark   # CI lane
bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-browser bench:miniwob           # oracle (writes artifact)
bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-browser bench:miniwob -- --policy noop
bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-browser bench:miniwob -- --policy wrong
```

## Deferred (tracked, not dropped)

The remaining #9476 "Needs CI infra" items — a real-Chromium engine lane, an external-dataset benchmark (Mind2Web/WebArena) through plugin-browser, a web-element grounding benchmark, and CI gating — are tracked in follow-up #10333. The adapter is already engine-agnostic, so the real-Chromium lane is a new executor + gated lane, not a rewrite.

Closes #9476.

## What

79:T1f46,Closes #10553

## Problem

`PayoutProcessorService.processBatch` was supposed to recover redemptions whose worker died mid-payout, but the recovery was **dead code**. It selected:

```
status = 'approved' AND (processing_started_at IS NULL OR processing_started_at < now - LOCK_TIMEOUT)
```

A locked row has `status = 'processing'`, and `markFailed(retryable)` always resets `processing_started_at` to `NULL` when returning a row to `approved`. So **no `approved` row ever has a non-NULL `processing_started_at`** — the `lt(processing_started_at, …)` branch is unreachable. A redemption whose worker was evicted mid-payout stayed in `status = 'processing'` **forever**: funds locked in `total_pending`, never retried, contradicting the file's documented "automatic retry up to MAX_RETRY_ATTEMPTS." The same dead pattern was duplicated in the unused repo method `getApprovedForProcessing`. Separately, the per-redemption loop had **no try/catch**, so one RPC throw aborted the whole batch.

The cron (`*/5 /api/cron/process-redemptions`, CRON_SECRET-gated) is live.

## Why a naive fix double-pays

You cannot just re-approve stuck `processing` rows. If the worker died **after** broadcasting the transfer but **before** marking completed, the tokens are already on their way — re-approving re-broadcasts and **double-pays**. Recovery must be able to tell "never broadcast" from "broadcast, awaiting confirmation."

## Fix

### 1. Record the broadcast hash *at broadcast time* — the load-bearing invariant
New nullable column **`broadcast_tx_hash`** (migration `0155`, additive `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`, documented rollback). It is written the **instant** the tx is broadcast — the moment `writeContract` returns, **before** `waitForTransactionReceipt`. It is distinct from `tx_hash`, which is only written on confirmed completion. This lets recovery prove on-chain state:

- `broadcast_tx_hash IS NULL` → the payout provably never left our process → **safe to retry**.
- `broadcast_tx_hash IS NOT NULL` → a tx may be in flight → **must reconcile on-chain, never re-broadcast**.

The **Solana** path is refactored from `sendAndConfirmTransaction` to `getLatestBlockhash` → sign → `sendRawTransaction` (capture signature) → **record** → `confirmTransaction`, so it records the signature pre-confirmation too. The invariant is therefore **uniform across EVM and Solana**.

### 2. Separate, provably-safe recovery selector (`recoverStaleProcessing`)
Runs at the top of `processBatch` over stale `processing` rows (`processing_started_at < now − LOCK_TIMEOUT`):

| Case | Condition | Action |
|---|---|---|
| Provably safe, retries left | `broadcast_tx_hash IS NULL AND retry < MAX` | Re-approve (clears lock, `retry_count + 1`); retried in the same batch |
| Provably safe, retries exhausted | `broadcast_tx_hash IS NULL AND retry >= MAX` | Mark `failed` (manual intervention) |
| **Broadcast, unconfirmed** | `broadcast_tx_hash IS NOT NULL` | **Left in `processing`**, raise a `high` ops alert for on-chain reconciliation — **never re-approved** |

Recovery **never auto re-broadcasts a payout that already broadcast**. `MAX_RETRY_ATTEMPTS` semantics preserved (recovery increments `retry_count` and is gated by it). Fresh locks (within the timeout) are untouched, so a live worker is never robbed of its row.

### 3. Per-redemption try/catch (`handleProcessingThrow`)
A throw no longer aborts the batch. The handler re-reads the row and is **broadcast-aware**:
- broadcast hash present → leave in `processing` (record the reason), **no auto-retry** (would double-pay);
- no broadcast hash → safe retryable failure (`markFailed(retryable)`).

`markFailed(retryable)` now also clears `broadcast_tx_hash`, so each retry starts from a clean never-broadcast state (it only reaches there when nothing transferred: pre-broadcast failure, EVM revert, or atomic Solana failure).

### 4. Dead-code removal
Removed the unused `getApprovedForProcessing` repo method (the duplicated dead stale-lock pattern) and now-unused imports.

## Safety invariant (why this can't double-pay)

> Recovery and the throw-handler **re-broadcast only when `broadcast_tx_hash IS NULL`**, and the broadcast hash is persisted **before** the confirmation wait on every network. A row that ever broadcast a transaction therefore carries a non-NULL hash (modulo a single-DB-write residual window, see below) and is routed to on-chain reconciliation, never re-approved.

## Evidence

Real-DB test suite (`payout-stale-lock-recovery.test.ts`) driving the **real** `processBatch` against in-process PGlite, stubbing only the chain clients (viem) and RPC/env helpers — the selectors, lock, recovery SQL, broadcast persistence, and try/catch all run for real:

- **(a)** stale `processing`, no broadcast hash → recovered, re-approved, paid out (`writeContract` called once, `retry_count = 1`).
- **(b)** stale `processing`, **with** broadcast hash → **not** re-approved, status unchanged, **`writeContract` never called** (no double-pay), ops alert raised.
- **(c)** a throw in one redemption doesn't abort the batch — the other redemption still pays out.
- **(d)** the broadcast hash is persisted **before** confirmation: when `waitForTransactionReceipt` throws, `broadcast_tx_hash` is set, `tx_hash` is NULL, status stays `processing` (no re-approve).
- **(e)** a fresh lock (within `LOCK_TIMEOUT`) is left for the live worker.
- **(f)** a provably-safe, retry-exhausted row is failed for manual intervention.

```
bun test src/lib/services/__tests__/payout-stale-lock-recovery.test.ts   → 6 pass / 0 fail
bun run --cwd packages/cloud/shared typecheck                            → no errors in changed files
biome check (changed files)                                             → clean
```
Migration `0155` validated on PGlite (applies, column present, idempotent re-apply); journal contiguous through idx 153. Existing `payout-status-resilience` / `payout-networks` suites still green.

## Deferred (justified)

Full **automated** on-chain reconciliation of broadcast-but-unconfirmed rows (look up the receipt by `broadcast_tx_hash`, then settle the ledger idempotently) is intentionally **out of this slice**. Those rows are made **safe** (never auto re-broadcast) and **visible** (left in `processing` + `high` ops alert), exactly as the issue endorses. Automated reconciliation needs idempotent ledger settlement against `markCompleted`/`redeemable_earnings` and is a separate, carefully-tested change.

There is a tiny **residual window** for the broadcast hash: the worker could die between the broadcast returning a hash and the single DB write that persists it (one statement, vs. the long confirmation wait that was the original exposure). This is the design the issue specifies ("the moment writeContract returns the hash, before waitForTransactionReceipt"). Closing it fully would require deterministic offline signing (`sendRawTransaction` of a pre-hashed signed tx) and is not in scope.

> ⚠️ Money path — please **maintainer-merge** (board #10561 protocol). Not self-merged.

7d:T1dd5,## Coding Cockpit — a mobile-first coding-agent view

Adds a developer-only `/cockpit` GUI view in `plugin-task-coordinator` that unifies the coding-agent workflow on one screen:

- **`CockpitModePicker`** (`@elizaos/ui`) — a per-session mode picker collapsing the sanctioned coding modes into one selector, with a Fast/Smart tier toggle for Eliza Cloud. Presentational; emits a `CockpitModeConfig`.
- **`CockpitNewSessionForm`** — a free-text goal + the picker; on submit it lowers the selected mode to an orchestrator create-task `providerPolicy` via `buildCockpitCreateTaskInput` and hands the parent a ready `CodingAgentCreateTaskInput`.
- **`CockpitView`** — composes the existing `OrchestratorRoomView` (the live task-room deck, already on develop) with the new-session form.
- **`CockpitRoute`** (`plugin-task-coordinator`) — the live container: polls `client.getOrchestratorRooms()` (the deck) and spawns via `client.createOrchestratorTask()`. Registered as a `developerOnly`, GUI-only `/cockpit` view through the standard manifest + view-bundle pattern.

All mode → `providerPolicy` lowering lives in pure functions in `packages/ui/src/components/cockpit/cockpit-modes.ts` (UI has no business logic). The three sanctioned modes:

1. **Eliza Cloud** — eliza-code on Cerebras, fast/smart tier (`gpt-oss-120b` / `zai-glm-4.7`)
2. **OpenCode** — Cerebras
3. **Claude / Codex** — via the TOS-safe subscription connector

The emitted `{preferredFramework, providerSource, model}` values match develop's `TaskProviderPolicy` and the create-task route's `asProviderPolicy` parser (`plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator/src/api/orchestrator-routes.ts`).

### My Runtimes (kept — it ships live)

Also includes **`MyRuntimesSection`** + **`MyRuntimesContainer`** + **`switchRuntimeNonDestructive`** — a local / cloud-dedicated / VPS-remote runtime switcher. The switch logic reuses develop primitives (`repointBaseUrl`, the `isTrustedRestoreApiBaseUrl` trust-gate, the agent-profile registry). It is **mounted live in Settings** (`registerSettingsSection`, system group) — not exported-but-unmounted dead code — so it ships as a working, tested part of this PR rather than being deferred. The non-destructive switch re-points the client in place (no `SWITCH_AGENT` dispatch, no draft-clear, no StartupScreen flash) and trust-gates remote runtimes (public URLs rejected; loopback/RFC1918/CGNAT/tailscale/same-origin allowed).

### What was stripped (and why)

1. **Dead "canonical" orchestrator service.** An earlier iteration had `plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator/src/services/cockpit-mode.ts` (~210 lines) + its test — a server-side lowering that was never exported or imported by any production code (only its own test), a dead duplicate of the UI's `cockpit-modes.ts`. **Deleted** (the UI mirror is the single source of truth). Orchestrator typecheck passes with zero dangling references.

2. **TOS-unsafe experimental picker options.** The `claude-experimental` / `codex-experimental` modes and the `experimentalEnabled` gate depended on a "stealth subscription inference" replay-proxy branch that has been **rejected and removed** from the repo. **Removed** the two experimental options, the `mode: "experimental"` config variant, the `exp` badge, and the `experimentalEnabled` prop that threaded through the picker → form → view (`CockpitRoute` never set it, so the gate was already permanently unreachable). Also removed the now-trivial `visibleCockpitModeOptions` helper and its barrel export. A regression guard in the picker test asserts the experimental testids stay absent.

### audit:app status — the one remaining gate

The repo's mandatory app visual audit (`bun run --cwd packages/app audit:app`) **could not complete in this isolated worktree.** It boots a live stack that builds the full renderer via `turbo`, which requires a complete per-worktree `node_modules` (turbo, `@types/node`, the full dep graph). This worktree shares the parent repo's `node_modules` (no per-worktree install), so the live-stack prebuild fails at `@elizaos/logger:build: TS2688 Cannot find type definition file for 'node'`. I did **not** fake it.

What I verified instead:
- The cockpit is registered in the audit's plugin view-case list (`packages/app/test/ui-smoke/plugin-view-cases.ts`) so the audit will walk `/cockpit` at every viewport once it can run.
- **Brand rules by code inspection:** the cockpit components use **only semantic tokens** — no blue, no hex/rgb literals, no `sky-`/`indigo-`/`cyan-`. Accent (orange) appears only on active/selected states (`text-accent`, `border-accent`, `bg-accent-subtle`). The single hover is on a **neutral-resting** card (`border-border hover:bg-bg-hover` — neutral→neutral-with-opacity); the active orange card has no hover override, so there is no orange→black violation. `text-destructive` is used only for the error alert.

**Running `audit:app` on a full checkout, filling `manual-review/cockpit.md`, and committing the `/cockpit` screenshots is the one outstanding gate before merge.**

### Coordination note

This overlaps two in-flight efforts — the **Orchestrator / task-UI redesign** and **Multi-account coding agents**. The cockpit deliberately reuses develop's existing orchestrator surface (`OrchestratorRoomView`, the orchestrator client methods, `TaskProviderPolicy`) rather than standing up a parallel one. Reviewer: please coordinate so this doesn't become a third parallel orchestrator surface — it's intended as a thin mobile-first composition over the existing deck + create-task path.

7e:Td15,## What & why

Closes the stability proof-gap in #10197: the restart/watchdog code existed but nothing **acted** on memory pressure and no test proved crash-recovery. This PR makes the agent **restart cleanly before an OOM** and proves the recovery/soak/guard behavior with real, runnable tests + a one-command umbrella that induces the failures and scores them.

No second restart mechanism is introduced — everything funnels through the existing `requestRestart()` → `RESTART_EXIT_CODE=75` → `run-node.mjs` supervisor (CLI) or the host relaunch entrypoint (device). The watchdog is **opt-in** (`ELIZA_MEMORY_WATCHDOG`, default off) so production boot is byte-identical until enabled.

## Deliverables

| Area | File | Proof |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Memory watchdog** | `packages/agent/src/runtime/memory-watchdog.ts` (+ `eliza.ts` wiring) | RSS ≥ threshold for N samples → `[MemoryWatchdog]` warn + `requestRestart()`, never `process.exit`. DI'd; **12 unit tests** (threshold, sustained-trigger, transient-spike debounce, one-shot, env floors, timer-driven). |
| **Supervisor crash-recovery** | `packages/app-core/scripts/lib/restart-guard.mjs` (extracted from `run-node.mjs`, behavior-identical) | **5 guard unit tests** + **2 real-process integration tests** driving the actual `run-node.mjs` with a fake child (real exit-75) → relaunch + crash-loop abort. |
| **Soak / leak detector** | `packages/agent/src/runtime/memory-soak.ts` | `runSoak()` forces GC + reports heap slope; **2 tests** prove it flags a retained-allocation leak and reports a steady workload as flat. Closes the hole documented in `plugin-lifecycle-leaks.test.ts`. |
| **Umbrella + scoreboard** | `packages/app-core/scripts/stability-suite.mjs` (`stability:suite`) | One command **induces** crash→recovery + a leak workload, writes `10197-agent-stability-scoreboard.md`, exits non-zero on any failure. |
| **iOS watchdog parity** | `packages/app-core/platforms/ios/App/App/AgentWatchdog.swift` (+ `AppDelegate.swift`) | Liveness poll + bounded relaunch mirroring Android `ElizaAgentService`. |

## Verification (real, this host)

- `packages/agent` typecheck: **0 errors**. `memory-watchdog.test.ts` **12 ✓**, `memory-soak.test.ts` **2 ✓**.
- `packages/app-core`: `restart-guard.test.mjs` **5 ✓**, `run-node-supervisor.test.mjs` **2 ✓** (real child processes).
- `node packages/app-core/scripts/stability-suite.mjs` → **exit 0**:
  - supervisor-recovery: 2 induced restarts, recovered, latency 4526 ms
  - crash-loop-guard: aborts after 6 spawns
  - memory-soak: leaking slope 3.82 MB/iter vs steady −0.10 MB/iter, peak RSS 122 MB

Full evidence + per-criterion status: `.github/issue-evidence/10197-agent-stability.md`.

- **Cloud chainsaw kill/OOM → health restored** — needs a k8s cluster.
- **Real-LLM soak trajectory** — needs a live model key/budget; `runSoak` accepts a real message-loop `workload` for that lane.

81:Tac3,## What this adds

Builds the **interactive spine** of the coding cockpit on top of the foundation shipped in #10419 (mode picker + new-session + My Runtimes). This is the part that makes it actually *replace the terminal workflow*:

- **Drill-in session pane** — tap a deck room → its live transcript (`useOrchestratorData` + `buildConversation` + `ConversationBlockView`) + the full `TaskInspector` control bar (pause/resume/fork/restart/validate/add-agent/stop-agent), lifted out of the workbench monolith so one room embeds cleanly.
- **Bubble → focused-agent binding** — adds `onSubmit` to `ViewChatBinding` + a short-circuit in `ContinuousChatOverlay.submitText`; while a session pane is open the one floating composer drives **that** task (`postOrchestratorTaskMessage`). The hybrid-driver UX.
- **Real-CLI terminal** — a Transcript⇄Terminal toggle in the pane mounting `PtyConsoleBase`/`PtyTerminalPane` over the task's PTY sessions (narrowed from `getCodingAgentStatus`).
- **Fast/Smart tier toggle** — for Eliza Cloud sessions: persists the `providerPolicy` + respawns on the other Cerebras model.
- **App-control intent** — "open the cockpit" / "my agents" matcher noun + `ORCHESTRATOR_CAPABILITIES` on the view.
- **Phone gating** — hides the local runtime on store/android-cloud builds.

## Also fixes a live bug on develop

`CockpitRoute` on develop (from #10419) still has the **B1 "spawn-nothing" bug** — "Start agent" created an idle task and spawned no agent. This PR fixes it (create **then** `addOrchestratorAgent` with the picked mode) + adds a regression test.

#10419 removed the experimental TOS-unsafe Claude/Codex picker options. This branch **re-adds them, gated** (hidden unless a dev arms `experimentalEnabled`) — per nubs's call to keep them dormant. **Your veto** — happy to drop them to match develop if you'd rather they stay out.

84:Tc7c,## What

Lands the first **"Needs CI infra"** item from #9476's deferred checklist
(tracked in #10333): a **real-Chromium engine lane** for the MiniWoB++
plugin-browser benchmark. The MiniWoB++ suite now runs end-to-end through an
actual browser, not just JSDOM web mode — the same engine-agnostic
`BrowserBenchmarkAdapter` seam, a new executor, a gated lane. Not a rewrite.

## How

- **New executor** — `src/benchmark/chromium-executor.ts`: a
  `BrowserCommandExecutor` (`engine: "chromium"`) that drives the same
  `BrowserWorkspaceCommand`s (`navigate`/`click`/`type`/`fill`/`check`/`press`/
  `get`/`snapshot`) against a real Chromium-family browser via **puppeteer-core**
  (already a plugin-browser dependency — same as the computeruse real lanes; no
  Chromium bundled). Each episode renders the task's routed HTML through request
  interception — no external network, exactly like the JSDOM lane's
  `network route`. The executable resolves from an env override → a
  Playwright-installed Chromium (the `bunx playwright install chromium` pattern)
  → system Chrome/Edge/Brave.
- **New gated lane** — `src/benchmark/__tests__/miniwob-chromium.real.test.ts`:
  excluded from the default unit config (root vitest excludes `*.real.test.ts`),
  run via `packages/test/vitest/real.config.ts`. Self-skips when no
  Chromium-family browser is resolvable, so it is a clean no-op where one is not
  installed. Asserts **both directions** on the real engine: the **oracle solves
  every task×seed** (reward 1, every step an `engine: "chromium"` command), and
  the **noop baseline fails** (reward 0) — so the reward is grounded in real
  rendered DOM, not hard-coded.
- **Scripts** — `test:real:chromium` (the gated lane) and
  `bench:miniwob:chromium` (screenshot + scorecard evidence capture).

## Evidence (real browser, on Windows)

`.github/issue-evidence/10333-browser-real-chromium/`:
- `scorecard.json` — oracle on a real Chromium: **6/6 tasks solved**, per-task
  real-command trajectory.
- Per-task **start / solved screenshots** of the real rendered pages — e.g.
  `05-click-checkboxes-solved.png` shows exactly the requested checkbox ("lemon")
  checked and the rest left unchecked: the reward criterion, visually confirmed
  in a real browser.

Locally: the gated lane passes (oracle solves every task×seed on the
Playwright-installed Chromium; noop fails). puppeteer-core's WebSocket CDP works
under Bun (unlike Playwright's fd-pipe on Windows).

## CI wiring (workflow-scope-gated — needs a maintainer)

I can't add the CI step from this environment (the token lacks GitHub's
`workflow` scope, which blocks `.github/workflows/*` over both git and the API).
A maintainer should add, alongside the CUA real lanes, a gated job that runs
`bunx playwright install --with-deps chromium` then
`bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-browser test:real:chromium` (exact step in a PR
comment). The lane is committed, runnable, and green locally.

External-dataset benchmark (Mind2Web/WebArena) through plugin-browser, a
web-element grounding benchmark, and the CI gating itself — larger external
datasets / runner work; remain tracked on #10333.

88:Tede,## What & why

Closes the remaining **code** work on #9899. The pre-forward TTFT root cause is fixed and live (Tier 1 auth cache + Tier 2 optimistic billing). The issue + `packages/cloud/api/docs/inference-hot-path.md` named one remaining step: the **DB-backed pending-charge + settlement ledger** that closes the three at-scale residuals a KV-only backstop *cannot*. This implements it behind `INFERENCE_BILLING_LEDGER="db"` (default `""`/`kv` ⇒ **no behavior change** anywhere until ops flips it), matching the soak-then-cutover discipline the issue itself prescribed.

## Residuals closed (point-for-point)

1. **Hard concurrent-overdraw bound** — admission is ONE atomic `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` + `SUM(pending)` statement; a same-org burst serializes on the row lock and cannot collectively overdraw (was: KV soft bound via threshold only).
2. **True exactly-once settlement** — `request_id` PK + atomic `UPDATE … WHERE status='pending'` claim ⇒ inline settler and cron sweep can never both charge; the debit funnels through the single audited `creditsService.deductCredits`. No more KV single-flight lock (was: near-atomic get-then-delete ⇒ rare double-bill).
3. **Age-ordered sweep drain** — `ORDER BY enqueued_at LIMIT batch` loop over a partial index, no silent `maxKeys` cap.

Plus `uncollected` is now a first-class auditable row state; the org self-heals onto the synchronous-reserve path on a refused debit.

## Rollout (default OFF, no prod change)

`INFERENCE_BILLING_LEDGER` is `""` in every env block (still requires `INFERENCE_OPTIMISTIC_BILLING="true"`). The KV path is **byte-identical** when the flag is unset and stays as the rollback target. Cutover = ship migration `0153`, flip to `"db"` in staging, watch `[InferenceLedger] uncollected …` + sweep stats, then prod.

## Evidence (real path, no larp)

**90 pass / 0 fail** across the touched + related suites, every test driving the **real SQL against in-process PGlite** (same pattern as the audited `credits-deduct-guard.test.ts`):

|---|---|
| `inference-billing-ledger.test.ts` | 15 — admission (overdraw bound, threshold, unknown org, +Inf, idempotent, concurrency) · settle (actual, **exactly-once**, settle(0), uncollected) · sweep (stale/young, inline-then-sweep, age-ordered multi-batch) |
| `inference-pending-charges-migration.test.ts` | 6 — journal registration + **real migration-file apply to PGlite** + idempotent re-apply |
| `inference-billing-fast-path` / `auth-context` / `auth-lifecycle` / `cache-failure` / benchmark | 52 — KV path, no regression |
| `credits-deduct-guard` / `credits-reconcile` | 17 — credit mutation, no regression |

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 50 × \$3 | \$100 | \$1 | 33 | \$99 | \$150 | **\$50** | ✓ \$1 |
| 20 × \$10 | \$100 | \$5 | 10 | \$100 | \$200 | **\$100** | ✓ \$0 |

8c:T9bf,Stacked on #10475 (base `fix/10469-secret-prompt-swap`). Completes the secret/PII swap layer end-to-end: **detection + verification + egress**.

## Detection — 16 validated classes (`pii-detectors.ts`)
Each is a regex + structural validator so false positives are rejected: `credit-card` (Luhn + brand), `iban` (mod-97), `ssn` (SSA rules), `ipv4` (octet range), `email`, `jwt`, `phone`, `mac-address`, `hex-secret`, and cloud/provider keys (aws/stripe/google/github/openai/slack, PEM private-key). `detectPii()` resolves overlaps (longest wins); per-class opt-out (`disabledKinds`) on top of per-value `exemptValues`.

## Security hardening — unforgeable session nonce
Placeholders are `__ELIZA_SECRET_<nonce>_<n>__`; restore + the exec-boundary assert are scoped to the session nonce, so a user/model can't forge a placeholder to hijack a real secret, and benign placeholder-shaped text isn't falsely failed. A this-session placeholder the model *fabricated* fails loud.

## Egress wired (the execution boundary) — gated, default-off, zero-cost when disabled
- **Turn-scoped session** on the AsyncLocalStorage trajectory context (spans ingress `useModel` → action exec). `useModel` mints it once per turn and reuses it so all calls share a nonce.
- **Single restore funnel**: `executePlannedToolCall` (every model-selected action — top-level/sub-planner/autonomy) restores real secrets into handler args just before `action.handler`; the model/transcripts/logs/trajectory keep placeholders. A fabricated placeholder **fails loud** — never sent to a real command/connector/endpoint.

## Tests — 131/131 green; `packages/core` typecheck + biome clean
Validity (21), **fuzz** (seeded: 4000 secret docs + 2000 unicode — round-trip/no-leak/deterministic/idempotent), **red-team** (10), **benchmark** (substitute+restore ~371 hz/2.7 ms @ 50 KB), **egress e2e** (3: real secret reaches handler; fabricated → fail loud; disabled → no-op), runtime integration + existing exec suites unchanged (28/28).

**3 real bugs the fuzz/red-team caught + fixed:** phone FP on bare digit runs; a credit-card regex that ate a leading stray digit, failed Luhn, and **leaked the card**; placeholder forgery/collision.

Evidence: `.github/issue-evidence/10469-pii-detectors-and-tests.md`. Remaining: a live-model trajectory (needs a provider key, unset here) — the path is proven deterministically with a real `SecretSwapSession`.

Follow-up to the #10195 candidate-finder: extends the finder with the detector class #10201 called out, **consolidates one high-confidence type family end to end**, documents triage, and adds an advisory post-cleanup baseline.

## What this does

1. **New detector — literal-set duplicates (class 4).** `type-duplication-audit.mjs` now clusters string-literal unions, `enum`s, and `as const` maps by their **value set** across packages — even under different names. It surfaces 211 families today (e.g. `LogLevel` = `debug|error|info|warn` in 6 packages; `RoleName`/`RequiredRole`/… = `ADMIN|GUEST|OWNER|USER` in 6 packages; the cloud-api status unions). Deliberate deferral (documented): zod↔type shape matching — heuristic/noisy.

2. **Consolidated family — connector-setup `SetupState`.** `SetupState` / `SetupStatusResponse` / `SetupErrorResponse` / `SETUP_ERROR_CODES` / `buildSetupError` / `setupPath` were declared verbatim in `@elizaos/app-core/api/setup-contract.ts` and **re-mirrored in 7 connector setup-routes + 3 data-routes files**. They now live once in `packages/core/src/types/connector-setup.ts` — the inward package every connector already depends on (and which already hosts `Route`/`RouteRequest`/`RouteResponse`). `app-core` re-exports them (import path unchanged); the per-connector `setupError` helpers collapse into the shared `buildSetupError`. `plugin-telegram` keeps a *specialized* `SetupStatusResponse` built on the shared `SetupState` (legitimate refinement). After the change `SetupState` occurs **0 times** in the candidate report (was a 6-package / 7-declaration cluster).

4. **Triage docs + decision log + allowlist.** `packages/scripts/type-duplication-triage.md` documents how to triage false positives, when **not** to consolidate (with the real reject cases: `CredentialProviderResult` intentional-inline, the cloud-api mirrored/vendored DTOs, the frozen `ScheduledTask` contract-types, standalone-package `JsonValue`), and records the accept/reject log. Reviewed-but-kept-separate families are allowlisted with reasons.

5. **Advisory baseline.** `type-duplication-audit.baseline.json` (written *after* the cleanup, per #10201) + `--check` (exits 0; `--strict` opt-in) make future drift visible without blocking local types. The cheap self-test is wired into `quality.yml` / `quality-fork.yml`; the full scan stays off the hot path.

Real macOS/Windows Electrobun, iOS/Android device live captures, openWakeWord real-head, Stage-B STT evaluation, and full hardware-backed screen/audio artifacts remain required for #9958 overall.8f:T1141,## #9142 Part 2 — single-frame visual-glitch e2e harness

Part 1 (the *static* invisible drag-handle regression) is already fixed and guarded. This adds the missing **Part 2**: a harness that catches **transient** glitches — a wrong state that shows for **one frame** mid-animation, which a post-settle screenshot can never see. The issue's reopen comment enumerates exactly this gap ("No frame-sequence glitch harness … no consecutive-frame pixelmatch … no deliberately-broken canary").

### What it does

`packages/ui/src/components/shell/__e2e__/run-chat-sheet-frame-glitch-e2e.mjs` (animations **ON**, no determinism shim):

1. esbuild-bundles the real `chat-sheet-fixture.tsx` (same approach as the existing `run-chat-sheet-e2e.mjs`), loads it in headless Chromium, and drives a **real pointer gesture** that opens the sheet from its collapsed pill.
2. Captures a dense **CDP screencast frame burst** across the spring transition (every painted frame — far denser than `page.screenshot()` polling).
3. **Single-frame flash detector** — `pixelmatch`es every consecutive frame pair. A monotonic animation diffs smoothly; a one-frame flash at frame *k* is an **outlier**: it differs hard from *both* neighbours while the neighbours agree with each other (`diff(k-1,k+1) ≪ diff(k-1,k), diff(k,k+1)`). Those *k* are flagged.
4. **"Two pills" crossfade guard** — every rAF, samples the **effective** opacity (product up the DOM tree) of the pill bar (`chat-pill`) and grabber bar (`chat-sheet-grabber`). The component crossfades them with **no overlap** (`pillOpacity` over openProgress [0,0.55], `grabberOpacity` over [0.55,0.95]); the harness asserts they're never both visible at once — the exact "two bars stranded on screen" bug.
5. `--canary` injects a one-frame wrong state into **both** detectors and asserts each **FIRES**, proving the harness is not a no-op.

Evidence (frame burst, per-frame diff overlay, opacity trace, summary, console log) → `.github/issue-evidence/9142-frame-glitch/`.

### Evidence

- **Detector math, validated on synthetic frames** (standalone, no browser): a smooth slide → **0 flashes** (no false positives); an injected one-frame flash → **flagged at exactly that frame**; a monotonic scene change that *stays* → **correctly NOT flagged** (distinguishes a transient flash from a real state change).
- **Live run (Windows 11, headless Chromium under node)** — real round-trip (flick down to the pill, flick up to open) that crosses the crossfade both ways:
  - **34 frames + 142 opacity samples** captured across the spring transition.
  - **crossfade exercised**: pill swept to 1.00, grabber swept to 1.00 (full anti-phase morph).
  - **0 single-frame flashes**; **worst pill+grabber overlap = 0.000** → the strict crossfade holds on `develop` (the "two pills" invariant is upheld). Stable across 3 consecutive runs.
  - **`--canary`: both detectors FIRE** on the injected one-frame wrong state — proving the harness is not a no-op.
  - Evidence committed: `frame-*.png` (the burst), `diff-overlay-peak-*.png` (pixelmatch per-frame diffs), `summary.json` (per-state trace), `console.log`. The open-sheet frame and the peak diff overlay were reviewed by hand (brand-faithful render; the peak diff is the legitimate thread-content slide, correctly not flagged).
  - **Robustness**: the harness hides the composer's blinking caret and gates flash detection to the active transition window — a first naive run flagged a caret-blink false positive in the static settle tail; both guards eliminate it without masking real (large-diff, mid-animation) glitches.
