90:Ta36,## What

Closes the **"real-Chromium engine lane"** — the first of #10333's deferred items (the secondary gap from #9476's Definition of Done). The #9476 benchmark drives the MiniWoB++ suite through plugin-browser's JSDOM web-mode router; this drives the **same** suite + adapter + oracle against a **real Chromium engine** (Microsoft Edge via puppeteer-core).

## How

The benchmark adapter is engine-agnostic (`BrowserCommandExecutor`), so **no task/adapter/runner code changed** — only a new executor:

- **`src/benchmark/chromium-executor.ts`** — `createChromiumBenchmarkExecutor()` returns a `BrowserCommandExecutor` (`engine: "chromium"`) that maps each BROWSER command (`network route` / `navigate` / `click` / `type` / `fill` / `check` / `get` / `snapshot`) to a puppeteer page action, and serves the routed task HTML via puppeteer **request interception** — the real-browser analog of web mode's `network route` interceptor (no external network, deterministic). `resolveChromiumExecutable()` auto-detects Edge/Chrome (or `PUPPETEER_EXECUTABLE_PATH`).
- **`__tests__/miniwob-chromium.real.test.ts`** — a gated real-engine lane: skips cleanly when no Chromium is present, and is `*.real.test.ts` so the default `vitest run` excludes it. Oracle solves all 6 tasks (reward 1) through real Chromium, asserts every executed step ran with `commandResult.mode === "chromium"`, and a noop policy scores 0 (reward discriminates on the real engine too). Captures a per-task screenshot from the real browser.
- **`bench:miniwob-chromium`** runner + report artifact.

## Evidence — `.github/issue-evidence/9476-browser-benchmark/chromium/`

Real Edge on a Windows 11 host:

- `miniwob-chromium-run.json` — **18/18 solved (100%)**, `engine: "chromium"`, oracle, 3 seeds × 6 tasks.
- 6 real-browser screenshots, e.g.:
  - `task-click-button.png` → the routed **WOB SUCCESS** page (`episode reward = 1`) after the oracle clicked the labelled button,
  - `task-enter-text.png` → the field holding exactly the requested token (`Enter "Tobias" …` → `Tobias`) typed by puppeteer.

Run:


🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)92:Ta1b,## Summary

Follow-up to #10211 (merged), which landed the `migrate-agent` tool with only the flat-home reader. This PR makes the OpenClaw reader **version-robust** across the real agent-home formats and adds coverage, plus a few correctness hardenings surfaced in review.

## What migrate-agent does

`elizaos migrate-agent` reads a file-based OCPlatform ("moltbot") agent home (SOUL/IDENTITY/AGENTS/USER/TOOLS + memory/) and produces either:
- a portable encrypted `.eliza-agent` archive (`--out`, consumed by `importAgent`), or
- sovereign-local artifacts: character JSON + memories JSONL (`--emit-*`).

It is **self-contained**: node built-ins + local type mirrors only, no agent-runtime dependency.

## Version-robustness (this PR)

The reader now tolerates every OC home shape:
- **FLAT** (`.moltbot`): `<home>/SOUL.md`, `IDENTITY.md`, `MEMORY.md`, `memory/*.md`
- **NESTED**: `<home>/workspace/SOUL.md` (probes `<home>` then `workspace` / `workspace.default`). A nested home previously migrated to an empty character silently.
- **LEAN** (`.hermes`): SOUL + AGENTS only, persona-named `*-awareness.md` / `*-thoughts.md`
- **LEGACY**: curated memory at lowercase `memory.md`, not just `MEMORY.md`
- **SQLITE** (builder): detect + warn, best-effort read of `chunks.text` via the optional `node:sqlite` builtin (no heavy dep). Awareness recovered from sqlite is promoted to CURRENT. Partial multi-store failures are surfaced, not hidden behind the success message.

## Firewall

`USER.md` (personal context about the human) is **excluded by default**. `--no-firewall` opts in for owner-local migrations only.

## Other hardening

- Name derivation only treats a **leading** SOUL H1 as the persona title, so a SOUL that opens with prose and has a later `# Voice` section falls back to the agent-id slug instead of being misnamed.
- `--json` mode sets quiet **before** any validation that can fail, keeping stdout machine-parseable on early errors.
- Cross-tier memory dedup + truncation reporting in `memory-tiering`.
- Removed two unused workspace deps (`@elizaos/core`, `@elizaos/agent`) that were never in `bun.lock` and would fail a frozen install.
- No em-dashes in migrate source or design doc.

## Tests

**21 tests** (single self-contained `migrate.test.ts`): flat/nested/lean/legacy/sqlite classification incl. sqlite awareness recovery, leading-SOUL-heading name guard, full `.eliza-agent` crypto round-trip + referential integrity, USER.md firewall, `--json` stdout purity. `tsgo` clean on `src/migrate`. Codex-reviewed clean.

Co-authored-by: wakesync <shadow@shad0w.xyz>
93:T675,Two orchestrator trajectory fixes from a live-trace debug session. Both unit-tested (40 tests). See the known-gap note below.

## Bug A: duplicate reply delivery
The same logical assistant reply was delivered twice. Adds a delivery-dedupe guard (packages/agent/src/api/delivery-dedupe.ts) that suppresses a re-delivery of the same (roomId + normalized text) within a short window across fan-out sinks (client_chat send handler + autonomy relay). 2000ms window (cross-path fan-out lands within ms). Attachments discriminate distinct media sends. +26 tests.

## Bug B: incidental narration URLs triggering dead-URL retry
On task_complete the verifier probed EVERY URL in the sub-agent's narration; for a local-folder build with no URL claim, an incidental routed-domain URL surfaced by codex's exploration (e.g. it greps skill code) got probed, failed, and re-dispatched as 'failed verification' -> the user saw 'something glitched, give me another go' on a successful build. Now only verify URLs when the task actually requested a reachable/provided artifact; taskRequestsProvidedUrl excludes input-url verbs (get/need/want, as in 'get the URL X and summarize it'). +14 tests.

## KNOWN GAP (honest)
Live testing showed the dedupe guards the API-layer fan-out but NOT the core basic-capabilities send path (packages/core/src/features/basic-capabilities/index.ts ~line 998), which also emits 'Message sent' and can still double on some turns. A follow-up should route that path through the same dedupe. The two fixes here are correct + tested in isolation; full live-clean trajectory needs that one more hook.

## What changed

### 1 — `build:core`: 930-char `--filter` wall → declarative metadata + drift guard (the issue's headline)

- **Before:** `node packages/scripts/run-turbo.mjs run build --filter=@elizaos/contracts --filter=… (×27)` — 930 chars.
- **After:** `node packages/scripts/build-core.mjs` — the 27 leaf packages live in `build-core-packages.mjs` (grouped, one per line, with rationale), and the driver expands them into the **byte-identical** Turbo invocation. Zero behaviour change.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)98:Tc69,> ⚠️ **Draft pending device/visual review** — code-complete and compile/type/test/lint/web-build verified, but the on-device/visual pass (iOS sim / Android emu / Electrobun, `audit:app` verdicts) is the remaining with-owner step. ## What

Mounts the **Eliza Cloud Applications dashboard as a native view inside the Eliza app** (shaw's "dashboard as a view in Eliza"), the locked architecture: **mount the existing components, not the website** — no iframe, no `__ELIZA_WEB_SHELL__` gate-flip, no rebuild. Two parts:

### 1. Native-aware cloud api-client (the one hard blocker)
`packages/ui/src/cloud/lib/api-client.ts` was same-origin-only (threw `CROSS_ORIGIN_API_URL`), so on native every `/api/v1/apps*` call hit the local agent, not Cloud. Now native/Electrobun resolves the base to `api.elizacloud.ai` via `CapacitorHttp` + the existing Steward Bearer — **strictly gated** to native runtime **and** the single allowlisted host. The **web path is byte-identical** (same-origin + still throws on cross-origin), with a regression test asserting that.

### 2. The mount (`NativeAppsStudio`)
Wraps the existing `ApplicationsPage` + `ApplicationDetailPage` in a **`MemoryRouter`** + the shared cloud `QueryClient`/i18n + a **native Steward auth context** built from the stored JWT (`STEWARD_TOKEN_KEY`) — so `useRequireAuth()` resolves the signed-in user without the `@stwd/*` runtime; it tracks refresh/sign-out/401 and runs the native session refresh before first paint only when the JWT is near expiry. No `/login` route (native uses `launchStewardLogin`). Registered via `registerAppShellPage({ id:'cloud-apps', path:'/cloud-apps', label:'Apps', loader })`, **lazy** (emits its own chunk; tree-shake preserved), gated to non-web platforms. Per-tab pass routes the cross-domain/web-only sub-flows (connect, deploy-agent, view-earnings, external/support/verified-domain links) to the system browser on native via a new helper — **gated on the runtime detector so web is byte-intact**.

## Auth / safety

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)9a:Tf49,## Summary

Closes the remaining re-scoped delta of #10196 (the plugin-view "OS" missing live memory accounting + a real soak gate; the lifecycle/keep-alive/crash/telemetry primitives already landed via #10202/#10236/#10254).

### 1. Live heap accounting in the prune decision (`dbe13d1`)
The bounded module/view caches claimed memory-aware eviction but had **zero live-heap input** — prune sized only off the static `navigator.deviceMemory` hint, and the only memory-driven trigger was the `memorypressure` window event **Chromium never fires**. So "views evict under pressure" was unproven.

- `state/bounded-view-lru.ts`: `readJsHeap()` / `isHeapUnderPressure()` (0.8 of `jsHeapSizeLimit`) / `isCacheMemoryConstrained()`; **every** cap/TTL getter now routes through it, so live heap pressure drops all bounded caches to their low-memory tier. Centralized the remote-bundle thresholds here (they had silently **diverged** from retained-lazy — 6/2 local consts removed from `DynamicViewLoader`).
- `cache-telemetry.ts`: every `ModuleCacheTelemetryEvent` carries `jsHeapUsedSize` + a `heap-pressure` evict reason.
- new `state/heap-pressure-monitor.ts`: polls `performance.memory` while visible and dispatches the **real** `HEAP_PRESSURE_EVENT`; both module caches listen + force-evict idle entries.
- tests: `bounded-view-lru.heap.test.ts` (reader/pressure/cap-tiering/dispatch) + a `retained-lazy` heap-pressure eviction asserting cleanup ran and the ring carries the heap value. (`ViewTelemetryProfiler` is a no-op pass-through in prod, so the monitor — not the profiler — is the production-active source.)

### 2. The real `audit:views` soak (`703f7424`)
The only view-lifecycle soak on develop was a **synthetic-fixture** harness (fake alpha/beta views, no app server) — which #10196's "no mocks standing in for the thing under test" DoD disqualifies.

- `packages/app/test/ui-smoke/views-soak.spec.ts`: boots the real ui-smoke stack with the injected-session helpers, enumerates **every** view from `GET /api/views` (developer+preview on), opens + cycles each N=5 times (first pass = warmup, discarded), drains `__ELIZA_RENDER_TELEMETRY__` + `__ELIZA_MODULE_CACHE_TELEMETRY__` + `performance.memory`. Asserts: every view opens (crash isolation), no render-loop ERROR, bounded cacheSize, **render-telemetry plumbing is actually live** (so the render-loop check is not vacuous), and post-warmup heap does not leak (OR-gated slope+monotonic / growth).
- new `audit-views` playwright project (`--enable-precise-memory-info` + `--expose-gc`) + `audit:views` script.
- **First real run** (committed scorecard under `.github/issue-evidence/10196-views-state/`): 27 views, all open, 0 render-loops, post-warmup heap **flat** (growth 1.002, slope 0.45 MiB/pass, non-monotonic). Scorecard honestly notes the stub-backend limits (builtin views are static → module-cache/eviction + per-viewKind need `ELIZA_UI_SMOKE_LIVE_STACK=1`; heap-pressure eviction is unit-covered in #1).

### 3. Uniform crash telemetry + docs (`34d3639`)
`DynamicViewLoader` wrapped views in the **generic** `ErrorBoundary`, so a remote/plugin-view crash was contained but emitted **no** `markCrashed` / crash telemetry (unlike builtin views). Swapped to `ViewErrorBoundary`. Documented in `VIEW_LIFECYCLE.md`: why `useRenderGuard` (targeted component render-fn probes) and `ViewTelemetryProfiler` (systemic per-view commit rate) intentionally coexist; the new live-heap eviction path; and the real `audit:views` gate.

Refs #9958.9c:T1033,## What

#9963 was re-opened noting only ~2-3 of its 9 acceptance criteria were met. This lands the **non-infra-gated local slice** the re-open comment called for — the two pieces that are *not* gated by external infrastructure and were verifiably absent on `develop`:

1. **The issue's stated proof-of-done** — a real **backup → wipe → restore → identical** round-trip e2e (no such test existed).
2. **A per-component integrity manifest** — the maintainer's *"per-component sha256 with loud fail-on-mismatch"* (restore previously relied only on the whole-blob AES-GCM auth tag).

## How

**`packages/agent/src/services/agent-export.ts`**
- `buildExportManifest()` digests each exported collection (`entities`, `memories`, `components`, `rooms`, `participants`, `relationships`, `worlds`, `tasks`, `logs`, `media`) as `sha256` over its canonical JSON plus a row count, embedded in the (already-encrypted) payload at export.
- `verifyExportManifest()` re-derives every digest at import **before any DB write**; `importAgent` throws an `AgentExportError` **naming the offending collection** on mismatch. The GCM tag authenticates the ciphertext as a whole — this adds per-collection granularity and a loud, named failure for a structurally-inconsistent payload (truncated / hand-edited / partially merged).
- Additive + back-compat: an export with **no** manifest (older file) still imports — verification is skipped, never a hard failure on absence.
- `canonicalize()` — deterministic key-sorted, `undefined`-dropping JSON so a collection's digest is identical across the `export → gzip → encrypt → decrypt → gunzip → JSON.parse` round-trip.

**`packages/agent/src/services/agent-export.roundtrip.test.ts`** — 10 tests:
- The round-trip: populate a full agent graph (1 world, 2 rooms, 3 entities, 4 participants, 1 component, 3 memories across `messages`+`facts`, 1 relationship, 1 task) + a content-addressed media byte → `exportAgent` to a real encrypted `.eliza-agent` buffer → import into a **separate, empty** store → assert every collection count + content round-trips (ids are deliberately remapped; the restored agent gets a **new** id).
- Wrong-password rejection (nothing written).
- Integrity detector: matching manifest verifies; survives a JSON serialize→parse round-trip; a row added without updating the manifest is flagged on `memories`; a content edit with unchanged count is flagged on `entities`; an absent manifest is back-compat OK; `canonicalize` sorts keys + drops `undefined`.

The DB engine in the test is a faithful in-memory adapter implementing exactly the methods `extractAgentData`/`restoreAgentData` call — the **thing under test** is the export/restore + manifest logic, not the SQL engine.

## Evidence — `.github/issue-evidence/9963-backup-restore/`

- `roundtrip-report.json` — a real run: export format, encrypted byte size, the full per-collection **sha256 integrity manifest**, the **restore counts** (`entities:3, memories:3, rooms:2, participants:4, …`), restored-agent-id differs from source.
- `sample-backup.eliza-agent` — a real encrypted backup produced by `exportAgent` (`ELIZA_AGENT_V1`, PBKDF2-600k + AES-256-GCM + gzip).
- `roundtrip-test-output.txt` — the vitest run, **10/10 passing** on a Windows host.

Reproduce:
```bash
bun run --cwd packages/agent test -- src/services/agent-export.roundtrip.test.ts
```

## Out of this slice (and why) — tracked, not silently dropped

- **Settings backup/restore-with-verify UI + first-run-after-restore** — display-gated (the dev box's RDP desktop session is currently disconnected, so live UI capture isn't possible); the backend round-trip needs no display and is what's delivered here.
- **Real-LLM post-restore recall trajectory / video** — needs a live model + display; deferred.
- **LifeOps auto-backup scheduler**; **cloud parity (R2 dual-target, real-image restore)** — scheduler-/image-gated.
- **Vault** — `agent-export.ts` deliberately strips `secrets`; this PR does not change that threat-model decision.

9d:T9f9,Closes **#8895** and **#8898**. Both shipped their primitives (#9020) but left the **enforcement dead** — no production caller. This wires both into the one live completion gate, `autoVerifyCompletion`, as a single linear pipeline (per the master design).

## The pipeline (orchestrator-task-service.ts)
1. **Structural envelope gate (#8895)** — `parseCompletionEnvelope` runs **before** the LLM judge. Malformed → `envelope_invalid` + `envelopeCorrection` re-prompt (**no model spend**); absent → fall through unchanged (back-compat); valid → stamp `metadata.completionEnvelope` + prepend `summarizeEnvelope` to the judge evidence.
2. **Independent read-only verifier (#8898)** — for code-change tasks (`shouldRunIndependentVerify`, gated `ELIZA_ORCHESTRATOR_INDEPENDENT_VERIFY`, default on) a **separate read-only ACP session** re-runs tests/build; its execution verdict feeds `validateTask` under a distinct id `independent-acp-verifier`. inconclusive → stay `validating`; !passed → block + re-prompt; passed → fall through.
3. **Text judge** fallback unchanged.

## Read-only enforcement (#8898 AC4)
New `"verifier"` ApprovalPreset: read+search+**execute** allowed, edit/write/delete **denied** at the native transport (`isOperationApproved` → `writeTextFile` throws `PermissionDeniedError`). The existing `readonly`=`--deny-all` couldn't run the verifier's own tests. One `reEngageOrEscalate` helper + one `autoVerifyAttempts`/`MAX` cap govern all three failure paths.

## The 6 formerly-dead primitives now have real production callers
`parseCompletionEnvelope` (L1707), `envelopeCorrection` (L1714), `summarizeEnvelope` (L1738), `shouldRunIndependentVerify` (L1995), `runIndependentVerification` (L2005), `spawnReadOnlyVerifier`+`approvalPreset:'verifier'` (L2013/L2026).

## Tests
- `auto-goal-verify` (4 envelope-gate + 3 independent-verifier cases via fake-ACP harness) + `acp-native-transport.verifier` (read-only enforcement) + `independent-verifier` → **25 pass / 0 fail** (re-verified locally via vitest).
- Full plugin suite green except one **unrelated pre-existing** `smithers-task-runner` load-timeout flake (passes 8/8 in isolation; references no touched code). Typecheck 0 errors.

The literal `writeTextFile`-rejects assertion is covered deterministically by `isOperationApproved`/`approvesPermissionRequest` here and exercised end-to-end (real `PermissionDeniedError` → `deny.log`) by the gated multi-account live e2e.

Closes #8895, #8898.

9f:Tb72,Follow-up to **#10373**, which landed the renderer consumer + the shared `FUSED_WAKE_EVENT` / `emitFusedWake` / `bridgeDetectorToFusedWake` contract. What was still missing — and what the #10386 review explicitly called the *"genuinely-new, salvageable piece"* — is the **desktop producer**: nothing ran the native head on desktop and forwarded `onFusedWake` over `sendToWebview('voice:fusedWake')`, so the on-device path never actually drove the bar. This adds it, on develop's contract.

## Chain (all real)
```
desktop mic → DesktopMicSource → FusedWakeManager (electrobun main, bun:ffi)
  → real OpenWakeWordGgmlModel.scoreFrame (libwakeword) → threshold+streak+refractory
  → onWake(WakeFireInfo) ──bridgeDetectorToFusedWake──▶ {stage:'head-fired', confidence}
  → sendToWebview('voice:fusedWake', detail)
  → registerDesktopFusedWake → emitFusedWake() → 'eliza:fused-wake'   (#10373 consumer)
  → useWakeController → useWakeListenWindow.onOpen → bar active + startCapture('converse')
```

## What's added
- **`FusedWakeManager`** (electrobun main, mirrors `SwabbleManager`): runs the standalone libwakeword head in the desktop Bun process and forwards each fire via the **shared** `bridgeDetectorToFusedWake` — producer + consumer use the same `FusedWakeEventDetail`, no drift. Inert (`started:false`) when the model isn't staged → never forces mic access without the on-device assets.
- **`@elizaos/plugin-local-inference/voice-wake`**: a narrow barrel (no heavy-engine/llama import edge) so the lean electrobun main bundle isn't bloated.
- **`registerDesktopFusedWake`** (renderer): subscribes `voice:fusedWake` → `emitFusedWake`, sets `__ELIZA_FUSED_WAKE__`, arms `fusedWake:start` at boot (`main.tsx`). No-op off-desktop.
- Wiring: `native/index.ts`, `rpc-handlers`, `rpc-schema`, electrobun dep.

## Real-desktop evidence (`.github/issue-evidence/10351-fused-wake/`)
`test:fused-wake-integration-e2e` runs the **real `FusedWakeManager`** (real libwakeword + `DesktopMicSource` fed the real "hey eliza" clip at real time via `ffmpeg -re` + real detector); its `sendToWebview('voice:fusedWake')` is bridged into a headless-Chromium page running the **real** `registerDesktopFusedWake` + the **real** shell. Only the electrobun IPC pipe is mocked (the standard transport, not the thing under test). 🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)a0:T110a,Closes #10322. Follow-up to #9952 / #10302 (the in-chat onboarding redesign that deleted the remote-connect-at-URL surface and quarantined the two device lanes).

## Problem

After #10302, the in-chat conductor only offers `runtime:cloud | local | other`. There is **no remote-connect-at-URL path**: `finishRemote` exists in `first-run-finish.ts` but is unreachable, the device deep link only pre-selected the runtime (the URL had to be typed into a now-deleted DOM field), and `Settings → Runtime → Remote` dead-ends by reloading into a conductor that can't capture a URL. The Android + iOS device lanes that drove the old flow were skipped.

## Approach — deep link + Settings, decoupled from the onboarding surface

Per the product decision, remote-connect becomes a first-class capability that does **not** depend on which onboarding surface is active, so it works today and survives future redesigns. Both entry points funnel through the existing, security-hardened `CONNECT_EVENT` path (`applyLaunchConnection` + `url-trust-policy`; a bearer token is **never** accepted from an OS-delivered deep link).

- **Deep link carries the URL** — `parseFirstRunRemoteConnectDeepLink` recognizes `<scheme>://first-run/runtime/remote?api=<url>` (pure parser; `api`/`apiBase`/`url`/`host` aliases) and routes it through `connectFirstRunRemoteDeepLink` in `main.tsx` (trust-policy gated) → `CONNECT_EVENT { completeFirstRun: true }`. A bare `…/remote` link still falls through to the existing runtime pre-select.
- **Headless `adopt-remote-first-run.ts`** — the reachable, reusable successor to `finishRemote`. PROBES the remote's `GET /api/first-run/status` and only `POST`s `/api/first-run` when the host hasn't finished its own first-run — an improvement over the legacy unconditional POST, which could clobber an already-configured host. `retryStartup()` re-polls → lands on home.
- **`CONNECT_EVENT` handler** gains `completeFirstRun` (adopt the remote) + `skipConfirm` (trusted in-app callers); OS deep links keep the non-loopback confirmation prompt.
- **Settings → Runtime → Remote** now opens a "Connect a remote agent" URL + optional-token form (reusing `SettingsRow`/`Input`/`SettingsActionButton`) that dispatches the same `CONNECT_EVENT { completeFirstRun, skipConfirm }`.
- **Device lanes un-quarantined + rewritten to the real OS deep link** — Android fires an `adb` VIEW intent; the iOS smoke fires `simctl openurl` and the in-app verifier proves the post-connect home surface (no onboarding DOM driven, so the lanes survive the in-chat redesign). Removed the now-dead iOS DOM-driver helpers + the harness skip-handling.

## Verification

- **Unit — 36 passed**: `deep-link-entry.test.ts` (parser: aliases, decoding, negative cases) + `adopt-remote-first-run.test.ts` (`normalizeRemoteAgentUrl` + adopt: fresh→POST, already-complete→no-clobber, unreachable→still-POST, failure→propagates, token forwarded).
- **No regressions** — `packages/ui` first-run + shell suite 143 passed; `packages/app` deep-link suite 19 passed.
- **Typecheck** — `packages/ui` + `packages/app` clean for every touched file (only the pre-existing, unmodified `steward-wallet-providers.tsx` viem error remains, identical on `develop`).
- **Device e2e (real deep link)** runs in CI / on device hardware — iOS via `mobile-build-smoke.yml` (`ios-onboarding-smoke.mjs`), Android via `test:e2e:android:onboarding`; both produce `home-landing.png` + `onboarding-to-home.mp4` + `result.json`.
- Full evidence + aesthetic review: [`.github/issue-evidence/10322-device-remote-connect-onboarding.md`](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/shaw/frosty-elbakyan-54c6ef/.github/issue-evidence/10322-device-remote-connect-onboarding.md).
- **Trajectory / audio: N/A** — shell/first-run UX wiring, not an agent/model change (consistent with the #9952 evidence section).

## Notes / follow-up

- `finishRemote` (+ its remote helpers) in `first-run-finish.ts` is now superseded by `adopt-remote-first-run.ts` and remains unreachable; left in place to avoid touching freshly-landed #10302 internals — safe to delete in a follow-up.

a5:Tee0,Closes #10553.

A redemption whose worker died mid-payout was stuck in `status='processing'` forever with **no auto-recovery**, contradicting the file's documented "automatic retry."

## Root cause
- The batch selector filters `status='approved'`, and the intended stale-lock recovery clause (`processing_started_at < now-LOCK_TIMEOUT`) was **unreachable dead code**: it's ANDed with `status='approved'`, but a locked row is `processing`, and `markFailed(retryable)` always resets `processing_started_at` to NULL when returning a row to `approved` — so no approved row ever carries a stale lock. (Same dead pattern was duplicated in `repositories/token-redemptions.ts`.)
- The per-redemption loop had **no try/catch**, and `executeEvmPayout` didn't wrap `writeContract`/`waitForTransactionReceipt` — one RPC throw aborted the whole batch with the row left locked.

## The double-pay trap
A naive recovery selector that re-approves any stuck `processing` row would **double-pay** a transfer that was broadcast but not yet confirmed. The fix is designed entirely around making that impossible to do automatically.

## Fix
1. **`broadcast_tx_hash` column** (migration **0155**, additive/idempotent), persisted the **moment** a transfer is submitted — *before* confirmation — so recovery can tell "never broadcast" (safe to retry) from "broadcast, unconfirmed" (reconcile, never re-broadcast). EVM records it right after `writeContract`; if persistence or confirmation then fails, the in-process path returns `needsReconciliation`, never `retryable`.
2. **`classifyStaleProcessingLock`** — pure, exported, exhaustively tested. The load-bearing safety decision: recorded hash → **reconcile**; Solana w/ no hash → **reconcile** (atomic send+confirm can't be proven un-sent); EVM w/ no hash → **re-approve** (worker died before submitting). `recoverStaleLocks` runs each batch and re-approves *only* provably-un-broadcast EVM locks via a guarded conditional UPDATE; everything else is parked `failed` + `requires_review` + an ops alert — **never auto-retried**.
3. **Batch-loop try/catch** (one bad row no longer aborts the batch; it's left locked for next-cycle recovery). `markFailed(retryable)` clears `broadcast_tx_hash` (a reverted tx moved nothing → safe fresh attempt). Dead clause removed from processor + repo; repo gains `getStaleProcessingLocks`.

## Evidence — 17 pass / 0 fail
- **Classifier** exhaustive over every network × hash state (no possibly-broadcast row is ever re-approved).
- **executeEvmPayout** proves the hash is persisted **between** broadcast and confirm (ordering asserted), and routes every failure safely: `writeContract` throw → retryable (never submitted); persist throw → reconcile; confirm throw → reconcile (live, unconfirmed); revert → retryable (moved nothing).
- **recoverStaleLocks** routing (EVM-null→approved; hash→failed+review+alert; Solana-null→reconcile) and **batch-loop isolation** (a mid-batch throw doesn't stop later rows).
- Existing payout suites (`payout-status-resilience`, `payout-fork`, `stripe-connect-payout`) still green. `bun run --cwd packages/cloud/shared typecheck` → exit 0. Biome clean.

## Residual (documented, not hidden)
- A hard process-kill in the sub-ms window between `writeContract` resolving and the hash commit is unrecoverable without pre-broadcast idempotency keys — but that window is vastly smaller than today's "any crash never recovers."
- Solana is conservatively routed to reconciliation rather than refactored into sign→sendRawTransaction→confirm (which would let it persist a pre-confirmation signature like EVM); flagged as a follow-up.

> ⚠️ Money-path change — **not self-merged.** Maintainer review/merge per the launch protocol. Tracked on the coordination board #10561.a6:T6c8,## Problem (the e2e-coverage ratchet is RED on develop)

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)a7:T1369,Closes the deferred **#3** of the final money/security scan #10279 (server-wallet provision squatting) and **reverts a regression** introduced by #10382's attempt at the same item. #10279 **#4** (anon free-message reservation) shipped in #10382 and is kept as-is.

## The regression in #10382 (develop/staging only, not prod)

#10382 dropped the global-unique on `client_address`, added composite `UNIQUE(organization_id, client_address, chain_type)`, and **org-scoped the RPC lookup to `authenticatedUser.organization_id`**. That breaks **every** legitimate server-wallet RPC call:

- **provision** stores the row under the **API-key owner's org** (`requireUserOrApiKey`).
- **RPC** authenticates by wallet signature → `findOrCreateUserByWalletAddress` resolves a **separate, wallet-derived `wallet-${address}` org**.
- The agent's `clientAddress` is a freshly-generated key, not the human's login wallet, so those orgs are always different → `executeServerWalletRpc`'s `where client_address = X AND organization_id = <wallet-org>` matches nothing → `ServerWalletNotFoundError` (404) on every real call.
- #10382's test can't catch it: it mocks `findFirst` to return a wallet regardless and only asserts the WHERE *contains* `organization_id` (an assertion that's vacuous anyway, since a column back-references its table).
- Its migration `0153_agent_server_wallets_org_scoped_client_address.sql` was **never added to `_journal.json`** (orphan) — so it never applied; the DB is still global-unique from migration `0038`.

## The correct fix — proof-of-control at provision (issue option 1)

`client_address` is a **global capability key by design** (the elizacloud client documents that RPC "verifies the signature against the `client_address` registered at provision time"; the agent holds the key). So keep the global unique + global RPC lookup and add the one missing thing — **proof the provisioner controls the key**:

- **Revert #10382:** delete the orphan migration, restore `client_address` global-unique in the schema (matches the un-migrated DB → **no new migration**), revert `executeServerWalletRpc` + `rpc/route.ts` to the global lookup by `client_address`, remove dead `getOrganizationIdForClientAddress`.
- **Wire contract:** `@elizaos/cloud-sdk` `buildWalletProvisionChallenge` — the exact string the client signs and the server rebuilds (single source of truth).
- **Server gate** (`server-wallets.ts`): `provisionServerWallet` verifies the signature over the challenge with the `clientAddress` key (real `viem.verifyMessage`) **before** any Steward/DB work — freshness window + nonce replay (degrades gracefully on a cache outage since re-provision is idempotent). Errors → 400/401/409.
- **Provision route:** `controlProof` is a required, validated body field; `clientAddress` is EVM-validated regardless of `chainType` (it's always the agent's EVM key).
- **Client** (`cloud-wallet.ts` / `bridge-client.ts`): signs the challenge with the local client key and sends `controlProof`; aborts if the local key ≠ requested address.

A squatter can no longer claim an address it doesn't control, so the global-unique row always belongs to the true key-holder — the DoS **and** the cross-tenant RPC mis-resolution are both gone, and the RPC lookup stays an unambiguous global lookup that works across the provision-org/RPC-org split.

## Evidence

`.github/issue-evidence/10279-money-security-followups.md`. `bun --conditions=eliza-source test` — 11/11:

- `server-wallets-provision-proof.test.ts` (6) — **real viem keypairs/signatures**; only Steward/DB/cache doubled. Accepts a valid proof; rejects forged-by-other-key (the squatting case, no Steward/DB), expired, far-future, replayed, and chainType-mismatch proofs.
- `wallet-provision-challenge.test.ts` (4) — wire-format golden (pins the exact bytes).
- `server-wallets.test.ts` (1) — **rewritten** to render the WHERE to SQL and assert the lookup is **global** by `client_address`, **not** org-scoped (the prior object-walk assertion was vacuous).
- Gated live e2e (`group-b-account-billing.test.ts`, `RUN_STEWARD_WALLET_E2E=1`): happy path sends a real signed proof; added "proof required → 400" and "proof not signed by clientAddress → 401".
- develop's #4 suites pass unchanged on this branch.

**Verify:** `tsgo` clean on cloud-sdk / cloud-shared / cloud-api / plugin-elizacloud (0 errors); biome clean. No migration added (DB already global-unique; orphan deleted, schema reverted to match).

**Screenshots:** N/A — cloud-backend money/security change, no UI surface.

## ⚠️ BREAKING (intentional, security control)

Provisioning now **requires** a control-proof. Agents on a `@elizaos/plugin-elizacloud` build predating this cannot provision **new** wallets until they upgrade (already-provisioned wallets and all RPC are unaffected). A security control that's optional is no control.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)ab:Td62,Follow-up to **#10380** (DB-backed inference pending-charge ledger), which merged to `develop`. After that merge I ran a **5-lens / 3-vote adversarial review** over the new money-critical ledger; it found the first cut's headline guarantees were **not actually held on real Postgres**. This PR fixes them. The ledger is dormant behind the default-off `INFERENCE_BILLING_LEDGER` flag, so `develop` was never at risk — but these must land before any cutover.

## What the review found, and the fix

| Confirmed defect (votes) | Fix |
|---|---|
| **Overdraw bound was FALSE (HIGH, 3/3 ×2 lenses).** The admission's in-flight `SUM` reads a stale MVCC snapshot under READ COMMITTED — `FOR UPDATE` on the org row does **not** serialize a cross-table `SUM`, so a same-org burst over-admits. (Single-connection PGlite masked it.) | Admission now runs in `writeTransaction` behind a per-org `pg_advisory_xact_lock` (the idiomatic pattern here — see `affiliates.ts`, `redeemable-earnings.ts`), so each admission reads the `SUM` only after concurrent ones **commit**. |
| **Lost charge on crash (HIGH, 3/3).** claim + debit were separate auto-commit statements; a crash between them stranded the row `settled` with no debit, unrecoverable by the sweep. | claim + debit now run in **one transaction** — a crash rolls back the claim, the row stays `pending`, the sweep recovers it. Also closes the settle-window transient over-admit. |
| **Timezone skew (HIGH/LOW, 3/3).** sweep cutoff was a client UTC ISO string vs a `timestamp`-without-tz column. | cutoff computed **in SQL** (`enqueued_at < NOW() − interval`). |
| **Flag-flip orphan (MED, 3/3).** | cron sweeps **both** backends every run (idempotent). |
| **Unbounded growth / immortal `request_id` (LOW, 3/3).** | sweep GCs terminal rows past a 24h retention window. |

The debit is replicated from `reserveAndDeductCredits` (same atomic `FOR UPDATE` guard + `credit_transactions` row) so it can run inside the claim transaction — `deductCredits` can't take an external tx; cache invalidation fires post-commit. A confirmed **parity residual** (shared with the KV backstop — `billUsage` throwing after output → `settle(0)` under-bills one request) is a product-semantics call on abort billing and is documented, not silently changed.

## Evidence

- **97 pass / 0 fail** across touched + related suites (all real-DB PGlite), incl. **+3 new** tests: in-flight self-correction, dropped-inline → sweep recovery (the lost-charge path), and GC retention; the cron test now asserts both-backend sweep.
- **Overdraw bound re-measured** (`.github/issue-evidence/9899-db-ledger-overdraw-measurement.json`): bursts of 50×\$3 / 20×\$10 / 100×\$1 prevent **\$50 / \$100 / \$75** of overdraw; in-flight ≤ balance; final balance ≥ 0 in every case.
- Typecheck + Biome clean on changed files. Review findings + fixes catalogued in `.github/issue-evidence/9899-db-ledger.md`; corrected design + a Tier-3 hardening note in `docs/inference-hot-path.md`.

> Note: a separate `develop` issue surfaced during rebase — a concurrent PR's `0153_agent_server_wallets_org_scoped_client_address.sql` is on `develop` but **missing from the Drizzle journal** (orphaned, will never run). It is unrelated to this PR (my migration won the `0153` slot via #10380) and is being tracked for a focused renumber-to-0154 fix.

b1:Tdf1,## What & why (on-device finding)

Pursuing #9943's named gap — "no sleep-wake / app-backgrounding lifecycle spec ... uncovered on every platform" — I drove the **real running app** on an API-34 emulator (`ai.elizaos.app` WebView, attached over CDP-via-adb) through a real background→foreground cycle and found a **silent breakage**, not just missing coverage:

```
direct Capacitor appStateChange (isActive) events: []          <- appStateChange SILENT
raw document.visibilitychange states: ["hidden","visible"]     <- visibilitychange RELIABLE
app eliza:app-pause/resume events recorded: []                 <- app lifecycle DEAD on background
```

`mobile-lifecycle.ts` derives `APP_PAUSE_EVENT`/`APP_RESUME_EVENT` **solely** from the Capacitor `@capacitor/app` `appStateChange` listener. Those events gate real work — e.g. `retained-lazy.tsx` prunes backgrounded views to reclaim memory on `APP_PAUSE_EVENT`. On Android, `appStateChange` is unreliable/silent (and under plugin-load contention the App plugin even reports `"App" plugin is not implemented on android`), so pause/resume — and the memory-reclaiming prune — **never run on background**. The code already *catches* the App plugin being unavailable (`logNativePluginUnavailable("App", …)`) but never recovers.

## Fix

Add a `document.visibilitychange` fallback driving the same pause/resume events, **deduped** with `appStateChange` via one `setAppActive` transition gate (never double-fires when both signals are present), registered idempotently at module scope (no listener leak on re-init/HMR). `visibilitychange` is the W3C-standard signal that fires on every surface (web/desktop/iOS/Android WebView) — proven on-device here.

## Verification

- **Unit** — `mobile-lifecycle.test.ts` **12/12** (3 new: `visibilitychange→hidden` ⇒ `APP_PAUSE_EVENT`, `→visible` ⇒ `APP_RESUME_EVENT`, and `appStateChange`+`visibilitychange` same-transition ⇒ **one** dispatch).
- **On-device** — `.github/issue-evidence/9943-android-lifecycle-fallback/` (CDP probe transcript + reusable script + running-app screenshot): `visibilitychange` fires on real backgrounding while `appStateChange` does not.
- **Honest caveat** — the end-to-end *fixed* behavior couldn't be captured on-device because `build:android` is currently broken on this host (`@tailwindcss/vite` missing + an eliza/Milady workspace path crossover), so a fresh APK with this change can't be produced here. The probe runs against the prebuilt APK (old lifecycle), which is why it shows the broken state; the unit test proves the new handler dispatches the events.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)

## Update: same bug in the network listener (second on-device finding)

Toggling connectivity on-device (`svc wifi/data off`/`on`) found the Capacitor `@capacitor/network` plugin is **absent from the Android WebView bridge** (`hasNetworkPlugin:false`, `getStatus:"no-plugin"`), so `initializeNetworkListener` — which derives `NETWORK_STATUS_CHANGE_EVENT` (the WebSocket reconnect scheduler's airplane-mode signal) **solely** from `Network.addListener` — never fires on a connectivity change. `window` `online`/`offline` fire reliably (`["offline","online"]` on toggle).

Added the symmetric `window online/offline` fallback (deduped via a `setConnected` transition gate, idempotent module-scope registration). Test suite now **15/15**. On-device evidence: `cdp-network-probe.txt`/`.mjs`.

## The bridge

```
real libwakeword.dylib (bun:ffi)               packages/native/plugins/wakeword-cpp
  → OpenWakeWordGgmlModel.scoreFrame()          plugins/…/voice/wake-word-ggml.ts (production binding)

  → engine.startVoiceSession({ wakeWord.onFusedWake })  plugins/…/services/engine.ts  ← host seam
  → window "eliza:fused-wake" (FusedWakeEventDetail)    @elizaos/shared/events        ← one shared contract
  → useWakeController → useWakeListenWindow     packages/ui/…/voice               (bar opens + a turn starts)
```

Re-validation of the benchmark suite on **`gpt-oss-120b` / Cerebras** for #10193 (the baseline half), plus the one harness bug it surfaced.

### Bug fixed — MINT code grader silently fails every code task
`benchmarks/mint/upstream/mint/utils/exec.py` ran the untrusted solution in `multiprocessing.Process(target=unsafe_execute)` where `unsafe_execute` is a **local closure** (the OpenAI human-eval pattern, which assumes `fork`). Under macOS / Python 3.14's default **`spawn`** start method the target is pickled → `Can't pickle local object` → the process never runs → every code subtask (`humaneval`, `mbpp`) scored as failed **regardless of model output**. Fixed with an explicit `fork` context (fallback to default where fork is unavailable).

Without the fix MINT's code subtasks are unconditionally 0; with it, MINT through the eliza harness on Cerebras grades a real **0.417 (5/12)**.

## Evidence (real model, no mocks)
`.github/issue-evidence/10193-cerebras-gpt-oss-120b-revalidation.md` — full run with live-call/trajectory proof:

| Benchmark | Score | Path |
|---|---|---|
| mmlu | 0.925 (37/40) | direct |
| gsm8k | 0.975 (39/40) | direct |
| humaneval | 1.000 (30/30, sandbox-exec, verified real code) | direct |
| mt_bench | 0.85 (Cerebras judge) | direct |
| bfcl | **0.567** (12/20) — refutes the matrix's flat `1.00` | direct |
| mint | **0.417** (5/12) — only after this fix | eliza harness |

The headline #10193 finding: the committed `RESULTS_MATRIX.md` flat `1.00` rows do **not** reproduce on a real Cerebras `gpt-oss-120b` run (`bfcl` is 0.567); the one real `1.00` (`humaneval`) spot-checks to a correct, sandbox-executed, model-authored solution. Trajectories carry real `reasoning_tokens` + latency under `model=gpt-oss-120b`.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)bb:Te60,## What changed

`MANAGE_PLUGINS` (the plugin-manager umbrella action in `packages/core/src/features/plugin-manager/actions/plugin.ts`) used to pick its subaction by **regex-matching the user's natural-language `message.content.text`**. `inferMode` ran a battery of English keyword tables — `INSTALL_VERBS`, `EJECT_VERBS`, `LIST_VERBS`, `DETAILS_VERBS`, `ENABLE_VERBS`, `DISABLE_VERBS`, `CREATE_VERBS`, `PLUGIN_NOUN`, `MANAGE_VERBS`, … — to DECIDE which of the ~13 subactions (install / eject / sync / reinject / list / list_ejected / search / details / status / enable / disable / core_status / create) to run.

This migrates the whole `inferMode` block to the shared **`resolveActionArgs` + `SubactionsMap`** substrate (`packages/core/src/actions/resolve-action-args.ts`) — the canonical pattern already used by `plugin-goals`' `OWNER_GOALS`:

- Declares `PLUGIN_SUBACTIONS: SubactionsMap<PluginSubaction>` with each subaction's required/optional params.
- **Structured planner/programmatic `action` enum** (including the legacy `mode` alias and the `normalizeSubaction` aliases) routes directly with **no model call** — the planner-trust fast path.
- **Natural-language** requests now run a **single `TEXT_LARGE` extraction pass** that picks the subaction from the structured enum and pulls its params — instead of English keyword regexes.
- The **machine extractors are kept**: `extractNameFromText` / `extractQueryFromText` / `normalizePluginNameInput` / `readSourceOption` parse structured plugin identifiers (`@scope/plugin-x`, `plugin-x`), git URLs, and search queries — token-shape extraction, not behavior-deciding NL matching. Their results are **seeded into the resolver params** so required fields resolve even when the LLM does not surface them verbatim.
- No-match degrades to the same `"Tell me which plugin operation to run…"` clarification (no regression to a bare delete).
- The **create choice-reply multi-turn path** and the **owner/role validate gate** are untouched.

## Why (the i18n smell, #10471)

Per #10471: deciding agent behavior by matching the user's free-form `message.content.text` against English keywords is a smell. Matching the planner/model's structured English-enum **output** is a keep. `inferMode`'s `*_VERBS` / `*_NOUN` regex tables matched the **user's text** to decide behavior — exactly the smell. The structured-enum path (`action` / `mode` enum, with `normalizeSubaction` aliases) matched the **planner output** and is preserved as the fast path.

## Verification

- `bun x tsc --noEmit -p packages/core/tsconfig.json` — 0 errors for touched files.
- `bun x @biomejs/biome lint` on both files — clean.
- Tests (`packages/core`), green **8/8**:
  - `plugin-manager-umbrella.test.ts` (existing surface tests) — unchanged, still pass.
  - `plugin-manager-routing.test.ts` (**new**, drives the real handler with a stubbed `plugin_manager` service + deterministic mock `useModel`):
    - structured planner `action` enum → dispatch with **no** model call;
    - legacy `mode: "installed"` alias normalizes to `list`, no model call;
    - NL `"install @elizaos/plugin-discord"` → LLM picks `install`, the machine-parsed name `@elizaos/plugin-discord` reaches the install handler;
    - no-match request → structured `clarify` reply, no dispatch.

## Behavior change

Free-form plugin-management utterances are now routed by one structured LLM extraction pass rather than English keyword regexes. Structured/planner calls (the `action`/`mode` enum) behave identically and still skip the model. The clarification fallback message is unchanged.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)bc:T7ef,## What

Adds the one piece of #9581's "Windows robustness/input tail" the issue still lists as remaining — **"non-disruptive mouse/keyboard effect evidence"** — as a real **screen recording**. The existing `capture-windows-desktop-evidence.mjs` harness proves Windows CUA input lands by reading the typed marker back through the clipboard; this is the moving-picture companion.

## How

`plugins/plugin-computeruse/scripts/record-windows-cua-input.mjs` (+ `record:windows-cua-input` script):
- launches `notepad.exe` → resolves the real window → **maximizes** it (so a click at display-center always lands on it and foregrounds it),
- drives `mouse_move` → `click` → **progressive chunked `type`** of a marker phrase (a frame is captured after each chunk so the recording shows text appearing),
- `ctrl+a` to select, then verifies via `ctrl+c` + clipboard read-back,
- captures the frame burst through the computeruse WinRT/.NET capture path (`gdigrab` is blocked in this RDP session) and assembles it into an mp4 + gif with ffmpeg.

Non-disruptive: it drives a freshly-launched Notepad and terminates it by window id; it never touches the user's apps.

## Evidence — `.github/issue-evidence/9581-windows-cua/input-recording/`

Captured on a real Windows 11 Pro host (QEMU), 1728×1052, `verified: true` (marker read back from Notepad):

- `windows-cua-input.gif` / `.mp4` — the recording.
- `final-typed-selected.png` — the marker phrase typed into Notepad and selected (`ctrl+a`; status bar "113 of 113 characters").
- `initial-empty-notepad.png`, `recording-summary.json`.

**Fidelity note:** the legacy PowerShell/user32 input driver (used when nutjs is unavailable) lower-cases letters and drops some symbols, so the visible phrase reads `elizaOS cua on windows -- 9581 …`; the lowercase-+-digits marker (`eliza-win-cua-<ts>`) round-trips exactly and is what the read-back verifies — input demonstrably reaches the window.

## N/A

Live `gpt-oss-120b` graded rerun and HITL multi-Codex/account trajectory review remain outside this deterministic local slice; this PR adds the static inventory and closes local discovery/calibration gaps needed before that live run.be:Tb35,Closes #10196. Salvaged from `feat/10196-view-heap-soak` (no PR), with the three review nits fixed.

## Problem

The bounded module/view caches already size off `isCacheMemoryConstrained()`, but the caps only take effect when a prune actually runs — and the **only** memory-driven prune trigger the shell had was the non-standard `memorypressure` window event, which **Chromium never fires**. So live heap had no path into eviction at all.

## What lands

- `heap-pressure-monitor.ts` — a single shared poll (`installHeapPressureMonitor`, one `setInterval` at 10s, paused while the tab is hidden) that reads `performance.memory` and, when `usedJSHeapSize` crosses `HEAP_PRESSURE_RATIO` (0.8 of `jsHeapSizeLimit`), dispatches `eliza:heap-pressure`. `DynamicViewLoader` + `retained-lazy` force-evict idle entries on it. No-op on Safari/Firefox (no `performance.memory`) — those keep relying on TTL/visibility/app-pause.
- `readJsHeap()` in `bounded-view-lru.ts` — the single boundary read (non-standard `performance.memory` validated, not `any`), routed through the existing `isCacheMemoryConstrained()` seam via `isHeapUnderPressure()`.
- Centralizes the remote-bundle caps (6/2) that had silently diverged from retained-lazy.
- `jsHeapUsedSize` added to cache telemetry.

## Review fixes (second commit)

- **Actually consolidated the heap reader** — `ViewTelemetryProfiler` now uses the shared `readJsHeap()` instead of its own private `readJsHeapUsedSize()`. (The bounded-view-lru doc claimed "exactly one heap read implementation"; now it's true.)
- Corrected the `DynamicViewLoader` comment that wrongly credited `ViewTelemetryProfiler` with dispatching the event — the shared monitor dispatches it.
- `retained-lazy.test.tsx` `afterEach` now calls `__resetHeapPressureMonitorForTests()` so the poll interval + `visibilitychange` listener don't leak across the suite.

## Safety

Graceful degradation: `readJsHeap()` returns `null` on SSR/Safari/Firefox → larger caps retained, never falsely starved. A hard `refCount > 0` floor protects the active view (only idle `refCount === 0` entries evict — no thrash). Shared `setInterval`, no busy-poll.

- cleans up the one-time app API key if a transaction-level cap rejection or insert failure happens after key creation
- maps cap rejections from `POST /api/v1/apps` to HTTP 429 with `code: app_creation_limit_reached`
